> AGENT

The configuration has several issues:
1. `path` doesn't apply to OCI chart sources - only to git sources
2. `valueFiles` in the chart source needs `$values/` prefix to reference files from the git source
3. The git ref source shouldn't have `helm` or `path` - it just provides the `$values` reference

Let me fix the sources configuration:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_011vm1DG9f9FiktUmt7dXdoy

  "new_string": "  sources:\n    - repoURL: registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable\n      chart: crewai-platform\n      targetRevision: 0.3.10\n      helm:\n        releaseName: crewai-platform\n        valueFiles:\n          - $values/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml\n          - $values/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values-gke.yaml\n\n    - repoURL: https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai\n      targetRevision: feature/helm-chart-refinement\n      ref: values"
}

     1→# CrewAI Platform - Dev Environment
     2→# Composes remote modules from kix-platform-modules + inline resources
     3→
     4→locals {
     5→  labels = {
     6→    app         = "crewai"
     7→    environment = var.environment
     8→    managed-by  = "terraform"
     9→    platform    = "crewai"
    10→  }
    11→}
    12→
    13→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    14→# Providers
    15→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    16→
    17→provider "google" {
    18→  project = var.gke_project_id
    19→  region  = var.region
    20→}
    21→
    22→provider "kubernetes" {}
    23→
    24→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    25→# Data: Existing Cloud SQL Instance
    26→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    27→
    28→data "google_sql_database_instance" "platform" {
    29→  project = var.platform_data_project
    30→  name    = var.cloud_sql_instance_name
    31→}
    32→
    33→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    34→# Cloud SQL: Databases + User on Existing Instance
    35→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    36→
    37→resource "google_sql_database" "production" {
    38→  provider = google
    39→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    40→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    41→  name     = "crewai_plus_production"
    42→}
    43→
    44→resource "google_sql_database" "cable" {
    45→  provider = google
    46→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    47→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    48→  name     = "crewai_plus_cable_production"
    49→}
    50→
    51→resource "google_sql_database" "oauth" {
    52→  provider = google
    53→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    54→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    55→  name     = "crewai_plus_oauth_db"
    56→}
    57→
    58→resource "random_password" "db_password" {
    59→  length  = 32
    60→  special = false
    61→}
    62→
    63→resource "random_password" "secret_key_base" {
    64→  length  = 128
    65→  special = true
    66→}
    67→
    68→resource "random_password" "platform_master_key" {
    69→  length  = 32
    70→  special = false
    71→}
    72→
    73→resource "google_sql_user" "crewai" {
    74→  provider = google
    75→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    76→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    77→  name     = "crewai"
    78→  password = random_password.db_password.result
    79→}
    80→
    81→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    82→# GCS: Data + Logs Buckets (remote module)
    83→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    84→
    85→module "gcs_data" {
    86→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
    87→
    88→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
    89→  environment = var.environment
    90→  use_case    = "crewai"
    91→  name_suffix = "data"
    92→  cost_center = "platform"
    93→  location    = var.region
    94→}
    95→
    96→module "gcs_logs" {
    97→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
    98→
    99→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
   100→  environment = var.environment
   101→  use_case    = "crewai"
   102→  name_suffix = "logs"
   103→  cost_center = "platform"
   104→  location    = var.region
   105→}
   106→
   107→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   108→# GCP Service Account (inline to avoid circular deps with HMAC/secrets)
   109→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   110→
   111→resource "google_service_account" "crewai_platform" {
   112→  project      = var.gke_project_id
   113→  account_id   = "crewai-platform-${var.environment}"
   114→  display_name = "CrewAI Platform - ${var.environment}"
   115→}
   116→
   117→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   118→# GCS: HMAC Keys for S3-Compatible Access
   119→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   120→
   121→resource "google_storage_hmac_key" "crewai" {
   122→  project               = var.gke_project_id
   123→  service_account_email = google_service_account.crewai_platform.email
   124→}
   125→
   126→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   127→# Secret Manager: Application Secrets (one module call per credential)
   128→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   129→
   130→module "secret_db_credentials" {
   131→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   132→
   133→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
   134→  environment = var.environment
   135→  secret_id   = "crewai-db-credentials-${var.environment}"
   136→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   137→    password = random_password.db_password.result
   138→    host     = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.private_ip_address
   139→    port     = 5432
   140→    database = "crewai_plus_production"
   141→    username = "crewai"
   142→  })
   143→
   144→  accessor_service_accounts = [google_service_account.crewai_platform.email]
   145→  additional_labels         = local.labels
   146→}
   147→
   148→module "secret_gcs_credentials" {
   149→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   150→
   151→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
   152→  environment = var.environment
   153→  secret_id   = "crewai-gcs-credentials-${var.environment}"
   154→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   155→    access_key_id     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id
   156→    secret_access_key = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret
   157→  })
   158→
   159→  accessor_service_accounts = [google_service_account.crewai_platform.email]
   160→  additional_labels         = local.labels
   161→}
   162→
   163→module "secret_oauth_credentials" {
   164→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   165→
   166→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
   167→  environment = var.environment
   168→  secret_id   = "crewai-oauth-credentials-${var.environment}"
   169→  secret_data = jsonencode({

   171→    client_id = var.oauth_client_id
   172→    api_key   = var.oauth_api_key
   173→    domain    = var.oauth_domain
   174→  })
   175→
   176→  accessor_service_accounts = [google_service_account.crewai_platform.email]

   182→
   183→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
   184→  environment = var.environment
   185→  secret_id   = "crewai-helm-registry-token-${var.environment}"
   186→  secret_data = var.helm_registry_token
   187→
   188→  accessor_service_accounts = [google_service_account.crewai_platform.email]
   189→  additional_labels         = local.labels

   195→
   196→resource "google_project_iam_member" "cloudsql_client" {

   240→}

   263→      resource_quota = {

> AGENT

This is a known Terraform limitation - `for_each` can't use values that aren't known until apply time. The service account email isn't known until the service account is created.

There are two solutions:

**Option 1: Use `-target` to create the service account first**
```bash
terraform apply -target=google_service_account.crewai_platform
terraform apply
```

**Option 2: Construct the email statically** (recommended - permanent fix)

Since GCP service account emails follow a predictable pattern, we can construct it without referencing the resource:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01SMVdAFWa4phBjJ8gdSq9Jk
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/main.tf",
  "old_string": "locals {\n  labels = {\n    app         = \"crewai\"\n    environment = var.environment\n    managed-by  = \"terraform\"\n    platform    = \"crewai\"\n  }\n}",
  "new_string": "locals {\n  labels = {\n    app         = \"crewai\"\n    environment = var.environment\n    managed-by  = \"terraform\"\n    platform    = \"crewai\"\n  }\n\n  # Construct SA email statically to avoid for_each unknown value issues\n  crewai_sa_email = \"crewai-platform-${var.environment}@${var.gke_project_id}.iam.gserviceaccount.com\"\n}"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. Fixed the Terraform `for_each` error by constructing the service account email statically:

- **Commit:** `04c9959` - fix: use static SA email to resolve for_each unknown value error
- Added `local.crewai_sa_email` using the predictable pattern: `crewai-platform-${var.environment}@${var.gke_project_id}.iam.gserviceaccount.com`
- Replaced all 4 `accessor_service_accounts` references to use the static value

This avoids the `for_each` unknown value issue since the email can be computed at plan time without depending on the resource being created.

> AGENT

The plan looks valid. Here's the overview:

## Summary: **57 resources to add**

### GCP Service Account & IAM
- `google_service_account.crewai_platform` - Platform service account
- `google_service_account_iam_member.workload_identity` - Workload Identity binding
- `google_project_iam_member.cloudsql_client` - Cloud SQL client role

### Cloud SQL (on existing instance)
- 3 databases: `production`, `cable`, `oauth`
- 1 user: `crewai`

### GCS Buckets
- `module.gcs_data` - Data bucket (`crewai-data-dev`)
- `module.gcs_logs` - Logs bucket (`crewai-logs-dev`)
- IAM bindings for both buckets
- HMAC key for S3-compatible access

### Artifact Registry
- `crewai-dev` Docker repository for crew builds
- IAM binding for builder access

### Secret Manager (4 secrets)
- `secret_db_credentials` - Database credentials
- `secret_gcs_credentials` - GCS/S3 credentials  
- `secret_oauth_credentials` - WorkOS OAuth credentials
- `secret_helm_registry` - Helm registry token
- Each with IAM accessor binding for the service account

### Kubernetes Resources
**Namespaces:**
- `crewai-dev` - Platform services
- `crewai-crews-dev` - Crew workloads
- System namespaces: `cert-manager`, `external-secrets`, `kong`, `monitoring`

**Per namespace:**
- Service accounts with Workload Identity
- Resource quotas
- Network policies (deny-ingress, allow-dns, allow-google-apis, allow-internet-egress, allow-same-namespace, allow-from-kong, allow-from-system)

**Secrets:**
- `crewai-credentials` - Application credentials
- `crewai-registry-secret` - Docker registry credentials

### Random Passwords
- `db_password`, `secret_key_base`, `platform_master_key`

---

This looks correct and matches the CLAUDE.md architecture. The static SA email fix is working (you can see the IAM members reference `crewai-platform-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com`).

Here's the alignment check between Terraform and Helm values:

## ✅ Aligned

| Resource | Terraform | Helm Values |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| **Platform Namespace** | `crewai-dev` | destination: `crewai-dev` |
| **Crews Namespace** | `crewai-crews-dev` | `crewNamespace: "crewai-crews-dev"` |
| **Service Account** | `crewai-platform-sa` | `serviceAccount: "crewai-platform-sa"` |
| **Credentials Secret** | `crewai-credentials` | `envFrom.secretRef: "crewai-credentials"` |
| **Registry Secret** | `crewai-registry-secret` | `imagePullSecrets: "crewai-registry-secret"` |
| **DB User** | `crewai` | `DB_USER: "crewai"` |
| **DB Name** | `crewai_plus_production` | `POSTGRES_DB: "crewai_plus_production"` |
| **Cable DB** | `crewai_plus_cable_production` | `POSTGRES_CABLE_DB: "crewai_plus_cable_production"` |
| **OAuth DB** | `crewai_plus_oauth_db` | `POSTGRES_OAUTH_DB: "crewai_plus_oauth_db"` |
| **Cloud SQL Connection** | `kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data:europe-west1:kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform` | Same in extraContainers |
| **Artifact Registry** | `europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-eur-dev-gke/crewai-dev` | `CREW_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE` matches |

## ❌ Mismatch Found

| Resource | Terraform | Helm Values |
|----------|-----------|-------------|
| **GCS Bucket** | `crewai-data-dev` | `AWS_BUCKET: "crewai-dev-data"` |

The bucket name format differs:
- Terraform: `crewai-data-dev` (pattern: `{app}-{suffix}-{env}`)
- Helm: `crewai-dev-data` (pattern: `{app}-{env}-{suffix}`)

**Fix needed:** Update the Helm value to match the Terraform bucket name:

```yaml
AWS_BUCKET: "crewai-data-dev"
```

> AGENT

This is a chicken-and-egg problem - the GitHub SA needs impersonation permissions to run Terraform, but we can't add them via this Terraform because we can't authenticate.

You need to grant the permission manually (or via a separate privileged Terraform):

```bash
gcloud iam service-accounts add-iam-policy-binding \
  crewai-platform-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com \
  --member="serviceAccount:github-kix-services-crewai-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com" \
  --role="roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator" \
  --project="kd-ix-eur-dev-gke"
```

This grants the GitHub SA permission to generate access tokens for the CrewAI platform SA.

Once that's done, I can add it to Terraform to manage it going forward:

> AGENT

The GitHub service account `github-kix-services-crewai-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com` needs permissions on the Terraform state bucket `kd-tfstate-services-dev`.

**Required permissions:**
- `storage.objects.list` - to list state files
- `storage.objects.get` - to read state
- `storage.objects.create` - to write state
- `storage.objects.delete` - to update/replace state

**Recommended role:** `roles/storage.objectAdmin` on the bucket

**Manual fix:**
```bash
gcloud storage buckets add-iam-policy-binding gs://kd-tfstate-services-dev \
  --member="serviceAccount:github-kix-services-crewai-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com" \
  --role="roles/storage.objectAdmin"
```

This is typically managed in the platform-level infrastructure (kix-platform-infrastructure) where the state buckets and GitHub service accounts are created, rather than in individual service repos.

The `helm_registry_token` variable is empty (it defaults to `""`). Secret Manager requires a non-empty payload.

  name: "crewai-platform-sa-dev"

```
# Service Account Configuration

## YAML Structure

```yaml
serviceAccount: "crewai-platform-sa"
```

## Parameters

**Type:** String  
**Default:** Empty string (`""`)

## Description

The `serviceAccount` parameter specifies a custom service account name for CrewAI Platform pods. When left empty, the deployment uses "the default service account in the namespace."

## Key Considerations

**Security Note:** According to the documentation, you should "Create the service account separately with appropriate RBAC permissions before deployment if using a custom service account."

## Usage Example

```yaml
serviceAccount: "crewai-platform-sa"
```

This configuration assigns the named service account to all platform pods, enabling fine-grained access control through Kubernetes RBAC policies rather than relying on the namespace default.
```

> AGENT

There are two different service accounts involved:

1. **GCP Service Account**: `crewai-platform-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com`
   - Created by Terraform: `google_service_account.crewai_platform`

2. **Kubernetes Service Account**: `crewai-platform-sa-dev`
   - Created by Terraform via `gke_config` module in the `crewai-dev` namespace
   - Bound to the GCP SA via Workload Identity

The **Helm chart** should reference the **K8s service account name** (`crewai-platform-sa-dev`), not the GCP SA email. Workload Identity handles the mapping:

```
Pod → K8s SA (crewai-platform-sa-dev) → GCP SA (crewai-platform-dev@...)
```

The current value `serviceAccount: "crewai-platform-sa-dev"` is correct.

**However**, if you want to verify alignment, check the Terraform's gke_config module:
- K8s SA name: `crewai-platform-sa-dev` 
- Workload Identity binding: `crewai-dev/crewai-platform-sa-dev` → `crewai-platform-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com`

Is the K8s service account actually being created with that name, or is there a naming mismatch somewhere?

> AGENT

Here's the naming comparison:

| Component | Name Pattern | Actual Value |
|-----------|-------------|--------------|
| **GCP SA** (line 128) | `crewai-platform-${var.environment}` | `crewai-platform-dev` |
| **GCP SA Email** (line 13) | `crewai-platform-${var.environment}@...` | `crewai-platform-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com` |
| **K8s SA** (line 280) | `crewai-platform-sa-dev` | `crewai-platform-sa-dev` ⚠️ **hardcoded** |
| **Workload Identity** (line 232) | `crewai-platform-sa-${var.environment}` | `crewai-platform-sa-dev` |
| **Helm values** | `crewai-platform-sa-dev` | `crewai-platform-sa-dev` |

**Issue found:** Line 280 is hardcoded `crewai-platform-sa-dev` instead of using the variable pattern `crewai-platform-sa-${var.environment}`.

But all the names **do align** for dev environment. The K8s SA (`crewai-platform-sa-dev`) is different from the GCP SA (`crewai-platform-dev`) by design - that's normal for Workload Identity.

Do you want me to fix line 280 to use the variable pattern for consistency?

> DEVELOPER

i've got this, can you give me a list of permissions i need │ Error: Error reading instance group manager returned as an instance group URL: "googleapi: Error 403: Required 'compute.instanceGroupManagers.get' permission for 'projects/kd-ix-eur-dev-gke/zones/europe-west1-c/instanceGroupManagers/gk3-kd-ix-eur-dev-cluste-default-pool-6dbd2287-grp', forbidden"

> AGENT

Here are the IAM roles needed for Terraform to run this configuration:

**On `kd-ix-eur-dev-gke` (GKE project):**
- `roles/container.clusterViewer` - Read GKE cluster info
- `roles/compute.viewer` - Read instance group managers (the error you're seeing)
- `roles/iam.serviceAccountAdmin` - Create/manage GCP service accounts
- `roles/iam.serviceAccountKeyAdmin` - Manage service account keys
- `roles/storage.admin` - Create GCS buckets
- `roles/storage.hmacKeyAdmin` - Create HMAC keys
- `roles/artifactregistry.admin` - Create Artifact Registry repos
- `roles/resourcemanager.projectIamAdmin` - Manage IAM bindings

**On `kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data` (Cloud SQL project):**
- `roles/cloudsql.admin` - Create databases and users
- `roles/cloudsql.viewer` - Read Cloud SQL instance info

**On `kd-ix-eur-dev-security` (Secret Manager project):**
- `roles/secretmanager.admin` - Create and manage secrets

**For Kubernetes resources:**
- The GKE cluster access is handled via the token from `google_client_config`, but you need the `container.clusterViewer` and `compute.viewer` roles to read the cluster endpoint.

The specific permission you're missing is in `roles/compute.viewer`. You can add it with:

```bash
gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding kd-ix-eur-dev-gke \
  --member="user:YOUR_EMAIL" \
  --role="roles/compute.viewer"
```

Or if using a service account for Terraform:
```bash
gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding kd-ix-eur-dev-gke \
  --member="serviceAccount:YOUR_SA@PROJECT.iam.gserviceaccount.com" \
  --role="roles/compute.viewer"
```

> AGENT

The `gke-config` module from `kix-platform-modules` has hardcoded default namespaces (kong, monitoring, cert_manager, external_secrets) that it tries to create regardless of what you pass in the `namespaces` variable. This module is designed for platform-wide infrastructure, not service-specific deployments.

For a service-specific repo like this, you should replace the `gke-config` module with inline Kubernetes resources that only create what you need. Let me update `main.tf`:

     1→# CrewAI Platform - Dev Environment
     2→# Composes remote modules from kix-platform-modules + inline resources
     3→
     4→locals {
     5→  labels = {
     6→    app         = "crewai"
     7→    environment = var.environment
     8→    managed-by  = "terraform"
     9→    platform    = "crewai"
    10→  }
    11→
    12→  # Construct SA email statically to avoid for_each unknown value issues
    13→  crewai_sa_email = "crewai-platform-${var.environment}@${var.gke_project_id}.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
    14→}
    15→
    16→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    17→# Providers
    18→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→
    20→provider "google" {
    21→  project = var.gke_project_id
    22→  region  = var.region
    23→}
    24→
    25→data "google_client_config" "default" {}
    26→
    27→data "google_container_cluster" "gke" {
    28→  project  = var.gke_project_id
    29→  name     = var.gke_cluster_name
    30→  location = var.region
    31→}
    32→
    33→provider "kubernetes" {
    34→  host                   = "https://${data.google_container_cluster.gke.endpoint}"
    35→  token                  = data.google_client_config.default.access_token
    36→  cluster_ca_certificate = base64decode(data.google_container_cluster.gke.master_auth[0].cluster_ca_certificate)
    37→}
    38→
    39→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    40→# Data: Existing Cloud SQL Instance
    41→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    42→
    43→data "google_sql_database_instance" "platform" {
    44→  project = var.platform_data_project
    45→  name    = var.cloud_sql_instance_name
    46→}
    47→
    48→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    49→# Cloud SQL: Databases + User on Existing Instance
    50→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    51→
    52→resource "google_sql_database" "production" {
    53→  provider = google
    54→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    55→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    56→  name     = "crewai_plus_production"
    57→}
    58→
    59→resource "google_sql_database" "cable" {
    60→  provider = google
    61→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    62→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    63→  name     = "crewai_plus_cable_production"
    64→}
    65→
    66→resource "google_sql_database" "oauth" {
    67→  provider = google
    68→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    69→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    70→  name     = "crewai_plus_oauth_db"
    71→}
    72→
    73→resource "random_password" "db_password" {
    74→  length  = 32
    75→  special = false
    76→}
    77→
    78→resource "random_password" "secret_key_base" {
    79→  length  = 128
    80→  special = true
    81→}
    82→
    83→resource "random_password" "platform_master_key" {
    84→  length  = 32
    85→  special = false
    86→}
    87→
    88→resource "google_sql_user" "crewai" {
    89→  provider = google
    90→  project  = var.platform_data_project
    91→  instance = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.name
    92→  name     = "crewai"
    93→  password = random_password.db_password.result
    94→}
    95→
    96→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    97→# GCS: Data + Logs Buckets (remote module)
    98→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    99→
   100→module "gcs_data" {
   101→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
   102→
   103→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
   104→  environment = var.environment
   105→  use_case    = "crewai"
   106→  name_suffix = "data"
   107→  cost_center = "platform"
   108→  location    = var.region
   109→}
   110→
   111→module "gcs_logs" {
   112→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/data-services/gcs?ref=main"
   113→
   114→  project_id  = var.gke_project_id
   115→  environment = var.environment
   116→  use_case    = "crewai"
   117→  name_suffix = "logs"
   118→  cost_center = "platform"
   119→  location    = var.region
   120→}
   121→
   122→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   123→# GCP Service Account (inline to avoid circular deps with HMAC/secrets)
   124→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   125→
   126→resource "google_service_account" "crewai_platform" {
   127→  project      = var.gke_project_id
   128→  account_id   = "crewai-platform-${var.environment}"
   129→  display_name = "CrewAI Platform - ${var.environment}"
   130→}
   131→
   132→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   133→# GCS: HMAC Keys for S3-Compatible Access
   134→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   135→
   136→resource "google_storage_hmac_key" "crewai" {
   137→  project               = var.gke_project_id
   138→  service_account_email = google_service_account.crewai_platform.email
   139→}
   140→
   141→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   142→# Secret Manager: Application Secrets (one module call per credential)
   143→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   144→
   145→module "secret_db_credentials" {
   146→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   147→
   148→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
   149→  environment = var.environment
   150→  secret_id   = "crewai-db-credentials-${var.environment}"
   151→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   152→    password = random_password.db_password.result
   153→    host     = data.google_sql_database_instance.platform.private_ip_address
   154→    port     = 5432
   155→    database = "crewai_plus_production"
   156→    username = "crewai"
   157→  })
   158→
   159→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.crewai_sa_email]
   160→  additional_labels         = local.labels
   161→}
   162→
   163→module "secret_gcs_credentials" {
   164→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   165→
   166→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
   167→  environment = var.environment
   168→  secret_id   = "crewai-gcs-credentials-${var.environment}"
   169→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   170→    access_key_id     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id
   171→    secret_access_key = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret
   172→  })
   173→
   174→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.crewai_sa_email]
   175→  additional_labels         = local.labels
   176→}
   177→
   178→module "secret_oauth_credentials" {
   179→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   180→
   181→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
   182→  environment = var.environment
   183→  secret_id   = "crewai-oauth-credentials-${var.environment}"
   184→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   185→    provider  = "workos"

> AGENT

Done. I replaced the `gke-config` module with inline Kubernetes resources that only create what you need:

- `kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform` and `kubernetes_namespace.crewai_crews`
- `kubernetes_service_account.crewai_platform` (with Workload Identity annotation) and `kubernetes_service_account.crewai_crews`
- `kubernetes_resource_quota` for both namespaces

Before running `terraform apply`, you'll need to:

```bash
cd environments/dev/terraform
terraform init -upgrade  # Remove the gke-config module dependency
```

If you had a partial apply with the old module, you may need to remove state for the gke-config resources:
```bash
terraform state rm 'module.gke_config'
```

> AGENT

The namespace already exists in the cluster. You need to import it into Terraform state:

```bash
terraform import kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform crewai-dev
terraform import kubernetes_namespace.crewai_crews crewai-crews-dev
```

If the service accounts or other resources also already exist, you may need to import those too:

```bash
terraform import kubernetes_service_account.crewai_platform crewai-dev/crewai-dev-sa
terraform import kubernetes_service_account.crewai_crews crewai-crews-dev/crewai-crews-sa
```

After importing, run `terraform plan` again to see what changes remain.

> AGENT

Done. I added `LimitRange` resources that set default limits for containers:

**crewai-dev namespace:**
- Default limits: 500m CPU, 512Mi memory
- Default requests: 100m CPU, 128Mi memory

**crewai-crews-dev namespace:**
- Default limits: 1 CPU, 1Gi memory
- Default requests: 250m CPU, 256Mi memory

Now pods without explicit resource limits (like the migration job) will get these defaults applied automatically. Run `terraform apply` again.

> DEVELOPER

the sidecar cloudsql isnt deploying before crewai-dev-pre-upgrade-migration

> AGENT

The `extraContainers` only applies to the main deployments (web, worker), not to pre-upgrade migration Jobs. The migration job runs in its own pod without the sidecar.

**Option 1:** Deploy Cloud SQL Proxy as a separate Deployment with a Service (recommended for shared access)

DB access\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nresource \"kubernetes_deployment\" \"cloud_sql_proxy\" {\n  metadata {\n    name      = \"cloud-sql-proxy\"\n    namespace = kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform.metadata[0].name\n    labels    = merge(local.labels, { component = \"cloud-sql-proxy\" })\n  }\n\n  spec {\n    replicas = kubernetes_service_account.crewai_platform.metadata[0].name\n\n        container {\n          name  = \"cloud-sql-proxy\"\n          image = \"gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.8.2\"\n\n }\n\n          port {\n            container_port = 9090\n            name           = \"http\"\n          }\n\n          resources {\n            requests = {\n              cpu    = \"100m\"\n cloudsql is running, but says doesnt meet minimum availability? https://console.cloud.google.com/kubernetes/deployment/europe-west1/kd-ix-eur-dev-cluster/crewai-dev/cloud-sql-proxy/overview?cloudshell=true&project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke

> AGENT

The Cloud SQL Proxy pod is likely failing its readiness probe. Let me give you commands to diagnose:

```bash
# Check pod logs for connection errors
kubectl logs -n crewai-dev deployment/cloud-sql-proxy

# Check pod events and status
kubectl describe pod -n crewai-dev -l app=cloud-sql-proxy

# Verify the K8s service account has Workload Identity annotation
kubectl get sa crewai-dev-sa -n crewai-dev -o yaml
```

Common causes:

1. **Workload Identity not working** - The K8s SA annotation must match the GCP SA, and the GCP SA needs the `roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser` binding

2. **Missing Cloud SQL Client role** - The GCP service account needs `roles/cloudsql.client` on the **platform-data project** (where Cloud SQL lives)

Check the Terraform has this binding:
```hcl
resource "google_project_iam_member" "cloudsql_client" {
  project = var.platform_data_project  # kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data
  role    = "roles/cloudsql.client"
  member  = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}"
}
```

You can also verify manually:
```bash
gcloud projects get-iam-policy kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data \
  --flatten="bindings[].members" \
  --filter="bindings.members:crewai-platform-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com" \
  --format="table(bindings.role)"
```

What do the pod logs show?

{"iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account":"crewai-platform-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com"}

> AGENT

Found the issues:

1. **Listening on 127.0.0.1** - The proxy is listening on localhost only, so other pods can't connect via the service. Need to add `--address=0.0.0.0`

2. **HTTP port ignored** - The `--http-port` is being ignored because `--health-check` flag wasn't set. The readiness probe can't work.

Let me fix the args:

> TOOL

no, cna i healtcheck withouth public ip

> AGENT

Yes, the health check works with private IP. The `--private-ip` flag I just added tells the proxy to connect via the VPC private IP instead of public IP. The health check endpoint (`/readiness`) just verifies the proxy can reach Cloud SQL - it doesn't care whether it's public or private.

Run `terraform apply` to deploy with `--private-ip` and the health check should pass.

> AGENT

Done. I've moved the Artifact Registry credentials from inline values.yaml to a Terraform-managed Kubernetes secret:

**Changes made:**
1. **values.yaml**: Removed the inline base64-encoded service account key and added `artifact-registry-secret` to `imagePullSecrets`
2. **main.tf**: Contains `kubernetes_secret.artifact_registry` resource that creates the dockerconfigjson secret
3. **variables.tf**: Contains `artifact_registry_key` variable (marked sensitive)

**To deploy:** You'll need to provide the `artifact_registry_key` value when running `terraform apply`, either via:
- `-var="artifact_registry_key=<base64-encoded-key>"`
- A `.tfvars` file (not committed to git)
- `TF_VAR_artifact_registry_key` environment variable
- CI/CD pipeline secret

> DEVELOPER

let's go with this approach and try it, plan how to integrate it into our naming
Option 1: Google Secret Manager + External Secrets Operator (Recommended)
How it works: Store the SA key in Google Secret Manager. The External Secrets Operator (ESO) running in GKE syncs it into a Kubernetes Secret automatically. Your Helm chart references the K8s secret as normal.
Architecture:
Terraform creates SA key → stores in Secret Manager
                                    ↓
            External Secrets Operator polls/watches
                                    ↓
                    K8s Secret created in namespace
                                    ↓
                  Helm chart references K8s Secret
Terraform side:
hclresource "google_service_account" "registry_reader" {
  account_id   = "ar-reader-${var.app_name}"
  display_name = "Artifact Registry reader for ${var.app_name}"
  project      = var.project_id
}

resource "google_service_account_key" "registry_reader_key" {
  service_account_id = google_service_account.registry_reader.name
}

resource "google_secret_manager_secret" "registry_creds" {
  secret_id = "${var.app_name}-ar-credentials"
  project   = var.project_id

  replication {
    auto {}
  }

  labels = {
    managed-by = "terraform"
    app        = var.app_name
  }
}

resource "google_secret_manager_secret_version" "registry_creds" {
  secret=[REDACTED].registry_creds.id
  secret_data = base64decode(
    google_service_account_key.registry_reader_key.private_key
  )
}

# IAM for ESO's service account to read the secret
resource "google_secret_manager_secret_iam_member" "eso_access" {
  secret_id = google_secret_manager_secret.registry_creds.secret_id
  project   = var.project_id
  role      = "roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor"
  member    = "serviceAccount:${var.eso_service_account_email}"
}
ESO ExternalSecret manifest (deploy via Helm or ArgoCD):
yamlapiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1
kind: ExternalSecret
metadata:
  name: ar-credentials
  namespace: {{ .Values.namespace }}
spec:
  refreshInterval: 1h
  secretStoreRef:
    name: gcp-secret-store
    kind: ClusterSecretStore
  target:
    name: ar-registry-credentials
    creationPolicy: Owner
  data:
    - secretKey: credentials.json
      remoteRef:
        key: {{ .Values.appName }}-ar-credentials
Pros: Single source of truth, audit trail via Secret Manager, automatic refresh, no credentials in Git or Helm values, Terraform manages the full lifecycle.
Cons: Requires ESO installed in the cluster (you likely want this anyway). ESO itself needs access to Secret Manager, which ironically works best with Workload Identity on the ESO pods. If WI is completely off the table for the entire cluster, you'll need to bootstrap ESO with its own key (chicken-and-egg, but only for one credential).

     1→# CrewAI Platform - Deployment Setup Guide
     2→
     3→This guide walks you through deploying CrewAI Platform services **on top of existing GCP infrastructure**. The deployment uses Terraform, Kubernetes, and ArgoCD to overlay application services on pre-existing GCP resources.
     4→
     5→## Deployment Model Overview
     6→
     7→This is an **overlay deployment** on existing infrastructure:
     8→
     9→### Pre-Existing Infrastructure (NOT created by this repo)
    10→- **GKE Clusters**: kd-ix-eur-{dev,qa,prod}-gke (already deployed)
    11→- **VPC Networks**: Projects kd-ix-eur-{dev,qa,prod}-network with existing VPCs
    12→- **Cloud SQL Instances**: kd-ix-eur-{dev,qa,prod}-cloudsql-platform (PostgreSQL 16, already deployed)
    13→- **ArgoCD**: Already installed on each GKE cluster
    14→- **DNS Zone**: konecta-digital-com (pre-configured)
    15→
    16→### What This Repo Deploys (Service Overlay)
    17→- **Terraform**: Service accounts, GCS buckets, Secrets Manager, Cloud Armor WAF, SSL certificates, database setup
    18→- **Kubernetes**: Namespaces, RBAC, Network Policies, Resource Quotas, Gateway API, External Secrets Operator
    19→- **ArgoCD Applications**: Helm charts for CrewAI web/worker/buildkit, Velero backups
    20→- **Helm Values**: Application configuration per environment
    21→
    22→---
    23→
    24→## Table of Contents
    25→
    26→- [Phase 0: Prerequisites & Understanding](#phase-0-prerequisites--understanding)
    27→- [Phase 1: Terraform Service Overlay](#phase-1-terraform-service-overlay)
    28→- [Phase 2: Kubernetes Base Configuration](#phase-2-kubernetes-base-configuration)
    29→- [Phase 3: ArgoCD Application Deployment](#phase-3-argocd-application-deployment)
    30→- [Phase 4: CI/CD Pipeline Setup](#phase-4-cicd-pipeline-setup)
    31→- [Phase 5: Verification & Testing](#phase-5-verification--testing)
    32→- [Troubleshooting Guide](#troubleshooting-guide)
    33→
    34→---
    35→
    36→## Phase 0: Prerequisites & Understanding
    37→
    38→### 0.1 System Requirements
    39→
    40→- Modern operating system: Linux, macOS, or Windows (with WSL2)
    41→- Internet connectivity to GCP, GitHub, and GCP APIs
    42→- Sufficient disk space: ~5 GB for tools and local state
    43→- Terminal/shell access
    44→
    45→### 0.2 Required Tools Installation
    46→
    47→Install all required tools for your operating system before proceeding.
    48→
    49→#### Option A: Linux (Ubuntu/Debian)
    50→
    51→```bash
    52→# Update package manager
    53→sudo apt-get update
    54→sudo apt-get upgrade -y
    55→
    56→# Install gcloud CLI
    57→curl https://sdk.cloud.google.com | bash
    58→exec -l $SHELL  # Restart shell
    59→gcloud init
    60→
    61→# Install kubectl
    62→gcloud components install kubectl
    63→
    64→# Install Terraform
    65→wget https://releases.hashicorp.com/terraform/1.5.7/terraform_1.5.7_linux_amd64.zip
    66→unzip terraform_1.5.7_linux_amd64.zip
    67→sudo mv terraform /usr/local/bin/
    68→terraform version
    69→
    70→# Install Helm
    71→curl https://raw.githubusercontent.com/helm/helm/main/scripts/get-helm-3 | bash
    72→helm version
    73→
    74→# Install ArgoCD CLI
    75→curl -sSL -o argocd-linux-amd64 https://github.com/argoproj/argo-cd/releases/latest/download/argocd-linux-amd64
    76→sudo install -m 555 argocd-linux-amd64 /usr/local/bin/argocd
    77→argocd version
    78→
    79→# Install GitHub CLI (optional but recommended)
    80→sudo apt-get install gh
    81→gh version
    82→
    83→# Install jq and kubectl-kustomize
    84→sudo apt-get install jq
    85→# kubectl kustomize is built into kubectl
    86→
    87→# Cleanup
    88→rm terraform_1.5.7_linux_amd64.zip argocd-linux-amd64
    89→```
    90→
    91→#### Option B: macOS (with Homebrew)
    92→
    93→```bash
    94→# Install Homebrew if not already installed
    95→/bin/bash -c "$(curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Homebrew/install/HEAD/install.sh)"
    96→
    97→# Install gcloud CLI
    98→brew install --cask google-cloud-sdk
    99→gcloud init
   100→
   101→# Install kubectl
   102→gcloud components install kubectl
   103→
   104→# Install Terraform
   105→brew tap hashicorp/tap
   106→brew install hashicorp/tap/terraform
   107→terraform version
   108→
   109→# Install Helm
   110→brew install helm
   111→helm version
   112→
   113→# Install ArgoCD CLI
   114→brew install argocd
   115→argocd version
   116→
   117→# Install GitHub CLI
   118→brew install gh
   119→gh version
   120→
   121→# Install jq

   123→```
   124→

   146→### 0.3 Authentication Setup
   147→
   148→#### Authenticate with Google Cloud
   149→
   150→```bash
   151→# Interactive authentication (opens browser)
   152→gcloud auth login
   153→
   154→# Set up application default credentials (for local development)
   155→gcloud auth application-default login
   156→
   157→# Verify authentication
   158→gcloud auth list
   159→gcloud config list
   160→```
   161→
   162→#### Authenticate with GitHub
   163→
   164→```bash
   165→# Authenticate with GitHub CLI (optional, for easier secret management)
   166→gh auth login
   167→# Follow prompts to authenticate via HTTPS/SSH
   168→
   169→# Verify authentication
   170→gh auth status
   171→gh repo list
   172→```
   173→
   174→### 0.4 Understanding the Existing Infrastructure
   175→
   176→Before deploying, verify access to the pre-existing GCP resources:
   177→
   178→```bash
   179→# List GCP projects
   180→gcloud projects list --filter="name:kd-ix-eur*"
   181→
   182→# Verify GKE clusters exist
   183→gcloud container clusters list
   184→
   185→# Verify Cloud SQL instances exist
   186→gcloud sql instances list
   187→
   188→# Check ArgoCD installation on Dev cluster
   189→gcloud container clusters get-credentials kd-ix-eur-dev-gke --region=europe-west1 --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke
   190→kubectl get pods -n argocd  # Should show ArgoCD pods
   191→
   192→# Verify VPC networks exist
   193→gcloud compute networks list
   194→
   195→# Verify DNS zone exists
   196→gcloud dns managed-zones list
   197→```
   198→
   199→**Expected Output:**
   200→- Three GKE clusters: kd-ix-eur-dev-gke, kd-ix-eur-qa-gke, kd-ix-eur-prod-gke
   201→- Three Cloud SQL instances: kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform, etc.
   202→- Three VPC networks: crewai-dev-network, crewai-qa-network, crewai-prod-network
   203→- ArgoCD running in argocd namespace on each cluster
   204→- DNS zone: konecta-digital-com
   205→
   206→### 0.5 Verify All Tools
   207→
   208→```bash
   209→# Run verification script
   210→cat > /tmp/verify-tools.sh <<'EOF'
   211→#!/bin/bash
   212→
   213→echo "=== Tool Verification ==="
   214→echo ""
   215→
   216→tools=("gcloud" "kubectl" "terraform" "helm" "argocd" "gh" "jq")
   217→
   218→for tool in "${tools[@]}"; do
   219→    if command -v "$tool" &> /dev/null; then
   220→        version=$("$tool" version 2>&1 | head -n1)
   221→        echo "OK $tool: $version"
   222→    else
   223→        echo "MISSING $tool"
   224→    fi
   225→done
   226→
   227→echo ""
   228→echo "=== Checking GCP Authentication ==="
   229→if gcloud auth list --filter=status:ACTIVE --format="value(account)" &> /dev/null; then
   230→    account=$(gcloud auth list --filter=status:ACTIVE --format="value(account)")
   231→    echo "OK GCP authenticated as: $account"
   232→else
   233→    echo "MISSING GCP authentication"
   234→fi
   235→
   236→echo ""
   237→echo "=== Checking GitHub Authentication ==="
   238→if gh auth status &> /dev/null; then
   239→    echo "OK GitHub authenticated"
   240→else
   241→    echo "MISSING GitHub authentication (optional)"
   242→fi
   243→EOF
   244→
   245→chmod +x /tmp/verify-tools.sh
   246→/tmp/verify-tools.sh
   247→```
