```json
  "content": "# ClusterSecretStore for GCP Secret Manager\n# Used by External Secrets Operator to sync secrets from GCP\napiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1\nkind: ClusterSecretStore\nmetadata:\n  name: gcp-secret-manager\nspec:\n  provider:\n    gcpsm:\n      # Project where secrets are stored\n      projectID: kd-ix-eur-dev-security\n      auth:\n        workloadIdentity:\n          # GKE cluster project\n          clusterLocation: europe-west1\n          clusterName: kd-ix-eur-dev-cluster\n          clusterProjectID: kd-ix-eur-dev-gke\n          # K8s service account used by ESO controller\n          serviceAccountRef:\n            name: external-secrets\n            namespace: external-secrets\n"
}
```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/kubernetes/overlays/dev/external-secret-ar.yaml",
  "content": "# ExternalSecret for Artifact Registry credentials\n# Syncs SA key from GCP Secret Manager and transforms to dockerconfigjson\napiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1\nkind: ExternalSecret\nmetadata:\n  name: artifact-registry-credentials\n  namespace: crewai-dev\nspec:\n  refreshInterval: 1h\n  secretStoreRef:\n    name: gcp-secret-manager\n    kind: ClusterSecretStore\n  target:\n    name: artifact-registry-secret\n    creationPolicy: Owner\n    template:\n      type: kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson\n      data:\n        .dockerconfigjson: |\n          {\n            \"auths\": {\n              \"europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev\": {\n                \"username\": \"_json_key\",\n                \"password\": \"{{ .ar_credentials | toString }}\",\n                \"auth\": \"{{ printf \"_json_key:%s\" (.ar_credentials | toString) | b64enc }}\"\n              }\n            }\n          }\n  data:\n    - secretKey: ar_credentials\n      remoteRef:\n        key: crewai-ar-credentials-dev\n"
}
```

> AGENT

Done. The ESO integration is now implemented. Here's the architecture:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                         TERRAFORM                                    │
│                                                                      │
│  google_service_account_key.ar_reader                               │
│              │                                                       │
│              ▼                                                       │
│  module.secret_ar_credentials (Secret Manager)                      │
│              │                                                       │
│              │ accessor_service_accounts:                           │
│              │   - crewai_platform SA                               │
│              │   - external_secrets SA                               │
└──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
               │
               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                    GCP SECRET MANAGER                                │
│                                                                      │
│  crewai-ar-credentials-dev (JSON SA key)                            │
└──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
               │
               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│              EXTERNAL SECRETS OPERATOR (ESO)                         │
│                                                                      │
│  ClusterSecretStore: gcp-secret-manager                             │
│       │ (Workload Identity → external-secrets-dev@)                 │
│       │                                                              │
│       ▼                                                              │
│  ExternalSecret=[REDACTED]                      │
│       │ - refreshInterval: 1h                                       │
│       │ - template: kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson                  │
│       │                                                              │
│       ▼                                                              │
│  K8s Secret=[REDACTED]                               │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
               │
               ▼
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│                     CREWAI PODS                                      │
│                                                                      │
│  imagePullSecrets:                                                   │
│    - name: artifact-registry-secret                                 │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Files created/modified:**

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| [main.tf](environments/dev/terraform/main.tf) | Added SA key, Secret Manager module, ESO GCP SA |
| [outputs.tf](environments/dev/terraform/outputs.tf) | Added ESO SA email and secret name outputs |
| [variables.tf](environments/dev/terraform/variables.tf) | Removed `artifact_registry_key` variable |
| [cluster-secret-store.yaml](kubernetes/base/cluster-secret-store.yaml) | ClusterSecretStore for GCP SM |
| [external-secret-ar.yaml](kubernetes/overlays/dev/external-secret-ar.yaml) | ExternalSecret for AR credentials |
| [application.yaml](environments/dev/argocd/apps/application.yaml) | Added kubernetes source |

**Prerequisites before deploy:**
1. ESO must be installed in the cluster (`helm install external-secrets external-secrets/external-secrets`)
2. Run `terraform apply` to create the SA key and Secret Manager secret
3. ArgoCD will sync the ESO manifests

> AGENT

I understand - you want to use the chart's `image.registries` format but pull credentials from the ESO-managed secret. This requires a few changes:

1. Update the ExternalSecret to output simple key/value pairs (not dockerconfigjson)
2. Use ArgoCD's `valuesFrom` to inject the secret values into Helm

Let me make these changes.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01VR26E5TqFjfuS8TjD4BCFz
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/kubernetes/overlays/dev/external-secret-ar.yaml",
  "old_string": "# ExternalSecret for Artifact Registry credentials\n# Syncs SA key from GCP Secret Manager and transforms to dockerconfigjson\napiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1\nkind: ExternalSecret\nmetadata:\n  name: artifact-registry-credentials\n  namespace: crewai-dev\nspec:\n  refreshInterval: 1h\n  secretStoreRef:\n    name: gcp-secret-manager\n    kind: ClusterSecretStore\n  target:\n    name: artifact-registry-secret\n    creationPolicy: Owner\n    template:\n      type: kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson\n      data:\n        .dockerconfigjson: |\n          {\n            \"auths\": {\n              \"europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev\": {\n                \"username\": \"_json_key\",\n                \"password\": \"{{ .ar_credentials | toString }}\",\n                \"auth\": \"{{ printf \"_json_key:%s\" (.ar_credentials | toString) | b64enc }}\"\n              }\n            }\n          }\n  data:\n    - secretKey: ar_credentials\n      remoteRef:\n        key: crewai-ar-credentials-dev",
  "new_string": "# ExternalSecret for Artifact Registry credentials\n# Syncs SA key from GCP Secret Manager for use with image.registries in Helm values\napiVersion: external-secrets.io/v1beta1\nkind: ExternalSecret\nmetadata:\n  name: artifact-registry-credentials\n  namespace: crewai-dev\nspec:\n  refreshInterval: 1h\n  secretStoreRef:\n    name: gcp-secret-manager\n    kind: ClusterSecretStore\n  target:\n    name: artifact-registry-secret\n    creationPolicy: Owner\n    template:\n      type: Opaque\n      data:\n        # Simple key/value format for ArgoCD valuesFrom injection\n        host: europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev\n        username: _json_key\n        password: \"{{ .ar_credentials | toString }}\"\n  data:\n    - secretKey: ar_credentials\n      remoteRef:\n        key: crewai-ar-credentials-dev"
}
```

> DEVELOPER

this format worked before, will this work with just SecretName
image:
  registries:

      username: "_json_key_base64"
      host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"

> AGENT

No, `secretName` likely won't work - that's not a standard field the chart expects. The chart wants inline `password`, `username`, `host` values.

For the ESO approach to work with this format, you have two options:

**Option 1: Use ArgoCD Vault Plugin (AVP)**
```yaml
image:
  registries:
    - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
      username: "_json_key"
      password: <path:projects/kd-ix-eur-dev-security/secrets/crewai-ar-credentials-dev>
```
Requires installing AVP in ArgoCD.

**Option 2: Keep using imagePullSecrets (simpler, works now)**

Revert to having ESO create a dockerconfigjson secret and reference it via `global.imagePullSecrets`:

```yaml
global:
  imagePullSecrets:
    - name: "crewai-registry-secret"
    - name: "artifact-registry-secret"  # ESO-managed
```

Which approach do you want? Option 2 is simpler and works without additional tooling. Option 1 requires AVP but keeps your preferred `image.registries` format.

> DEVELOPER

cna i get them from GCP Secret manager easily and insert

> AGENT

Yes, a few options:

**Quick manual fetch (for testing):**
```bash
# Get the SA key from Secret Manager
gcloud secrets versions access latest \
  --secret=[REDACTED] \
  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-security | base64 -w0
```

Then use in values.yaml:
```yaml
image:
  registries:
    - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
      username: "_json_key_base64"
      password: "<output-from-above>"
```

**Better: ArgoCD Vault Plugin (AVP)**

Install AVP, then in values.yaml:
```yaml
image:
  registries:
    - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
      username: "_json_key_base64"
      password: <path:projects/kd-ix-eur-dev-security/secrets/crewai-ar-credentials-dev#value | base64encode>
```

AVP replaces the placeholder at render time.

**Simplest with current ESO setup:**

Revert to `imagePullSecrets` which works with ESO-created dockerconfigjson secrets - no plugin needed.

Which do you prefer? I can set up AVP if you want the `image.registries` format with dynamic secret injection.

```json
  "old_string": "# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Artifact Registry: Service Account Key for Image Pulling (synced via ESO)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# Create a service account key for AR access (used by pods via ESO)\nresource \"google_service_account_key\" \"ar_reader\" {\n  service_account_id = google_service_account.crewai_platform.name\n}\n\n# Store the SA key in Secret Manager (ESO will sync to K8s)\nmodule \"secret_ar_credentials\" {\n  source = \"github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main\"\n\n  project_id  = var.security_project_id\n  environment = var.environment\n  secret_id   = \"crewai-ar-credentials-${var.environment}\"\n  secret_data = base64decode(google_service_account_key.ar_reader.private_key)\n\n  # ESO SA needs access to read this secret\n  accessor_service_accounts = [\n    local.crewai_sa_email,\n    \"external-secrets-${var.environment}@${var.gke_project_id}.iam.gserviceaccount.com\"\n  ]\n  additional_labels = local.labels\n}\n\n# Grant AR reader role to the platform SA (for pulling images)\nresource \"google_artifact_registry_repository_iam_member\" \"platform_reader\" {\n  project    = var.gke_project_id\n  location   = var.region\n  repository = google_artifact_registry_repository.crewai_builder.name\n  role       = \"roles/artifactregistry.reader\"\n  member     = \"serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}\"\n}\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# External Secrets Operator: GCP Service Account with Workload Identity\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\nresource \"google_service_account\" \"external_secrets\" {\n  project      = var.gke_project_id\n  account_id   = \"external-secrets-${var.environment}\"\n  display_name = \"External Secrets Operator - ${var.environment}\"\n}\n\n# Workload Identity binding for ESO\nresource \"google_service_account_iam_member\" \"eso_workload_identity\" {\n  service_account_id = google_service_account.external_secrets.name\n  role               = \"roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser\"\n  member             = \"serviceAccount:${var.gke_project_id}.svc.id.goog[external-secrets/external-secrets]\"\n}\n\n# Grant ESO SA permission to access Secret Manager secrets\nresource \"google_project_iam_member\" \"eso_secret_accessor\" {\n  project = var.security_project_id\n  role    = \"roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor\"\n  member  = \"serviceAccount:${google_service_account.external_secrets.email}\"\n}",
  "new_string": "# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Artifact Registry: Service Account Key for Image Pulling\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# Create a service account key for AR access\nresource \"google_service_account_key\" \"ar_reader\" {\n  service_account_id = google_service_account.crewai_platform.name\n}\n\n# Grant AR reader role to the platform SA (for pulling images)\nresource \"google_artifact_registry_repository_iam_member\" \"platform_reader\" {\n  project    = var.gke_project_id\n  location   = var.region\n  repository = google_artifact_registry_repository.crewai_builder.name\n  role       = \"roles/artifactregistry.reader\"\n  member     = \"serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}\"\n}"
}
```

**Cleaned up:**
- Removed ESO manifests (kubernetes/)

The SA key is now generated by Terraform and output directly - just paste it into values.yaml.

> DEVELOPER

check why im getting this? │ Error: Error creating service account key: googleapi: Error 403: Permission 'iam.serviceAccountKeys.create' denied on resource due to an IAM deny policy.

> AGENT

Your organization has an IAM deny policy blocking service account key creation - this is a security best practice many orgs enforce.

**Two options:**

**Option 1: Use Workload Identity (recommended)**

The GKE nodes can already pull from Artifact Registry using Workload Identity - no SA key needed. The platform SA already has `artifactregistry.reader` role.

Remove the SA key resource from Terraform and configure the chart to use Workload Identity for image pulling instead of `image.registries` credentials.

**Option 2: Request exception**

Ask your security/platform team to either:
- Exempt the `crewai-platform-dev` SA from the deny policy
- Pre-create the key and provide it to you

---

**For Option 1 (Workload Identity)**, I can update the config to remove the SA key and rely on Workload Identity. The CrewAI chart would need to ensure pods use the service account that has the Workload Identity binding.

Which approach do you want to take?

> DEVELOPER

it supports "ecr-login" - AWS ECR (requires IRSA or instance role) would this work on GCP?

> AGENT

Yes! GCP has an equivalent - if the chart supports `ecr-login` for AWS IRSA, it likely supports (or could support) GCP Workload Identity.

**GCP equivalent approaches:**

1. **`gcr` credential helper** - Similar to `ecr-login`, uses Workload Identity
2. **No explicit config needed** - GKE nodes with Workload Identity can pull from AR automatically if the SA has permissions

**Check if the chart supports:**
- `gcr` or `gcloud` as a registry type
- A generic "workload-identity" or "cloud-auth" option
- Or just leaving credentials empty and relying on node-level auth

**For your setup, it should "just work" because:**
1. Your K8s SA (`crewai-dev-sa`) has Workload Identity binding to GCP SA
2. GCP SA has `artifactregistry.reader` role (we added this)
3. Pods using that SA can authenticate automatically

**Try this in values.yaml:**
```yaml
image:
  registries:
    - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
      # Leave username/password empty - use Workload Identity
```

Or look for a config like:
```yaml
image:
  registries:
    - host: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev"
      authType: "gcr"  # or "workload-identity"
```

What options does the chart documentation show for `image.registries`?

> DEVELOPER

i need to add a docker-registry secret where should i put it

> AGENT

For which registry? You have two options:

**In Terraform (recommended)** - alongside existing secrets in [main.tf](environments/dev/terraform/main.tf):

```hcl
resource "kubernetes_secret" "artifact_registry" {
  metadata {
    name      = "artifact-registry-secret"
    namespace = kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform.metadata[0].name
    labels    = local.labels
  }

  type = "kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson"

  data = {
    ".dockerconfigjson" = jsonencode({
      auths = {
        "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev" = {
          username = "_json_key_base64"
          password = var.artifact_registry_key
        }
      }
    })
  }
}
```

Then add variable in [variables.tf](environments/dev/terraform/variables.tf):
```hcl
variable "artifact_registry_key" {
  description = "Base64-encoded SA key for Artifact Registry"
  type        = string
  sensitive   = true
}
```

**Or via kubectl (quick test):**
```bash
kubectl create secret docker-registry artifact-registry-secret \
  --docker-server=europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev \
  --docker-username=_json_key_base64 \
  --docker-password="<base64-key>" \
  -n crewai-dev
```

Which registry is this for and do you have the credentials?

> DEVELOPER

this is for a an image pull secret for the crewai registry

> AGENT

The secret already exists in Terraform! Look at [main.tf:426-444](environments/dev/terraform/main.tf#L426-L444) - `kubernetes_secret.helm_registry` creates `crewai-registry-secret`.

You just need to provide the `helm_registry_token` variable when running terraform:

```bash
terraform apply -var="helm_registry_token=<your-crewai-registry-token>"
```

Or add it to a `.tfvars` file (not committed):
```hcl
# dev.tfvars
helm_registry_token=[REDACTED]"
```

Then:
```bash
terraform apply -var-file="dev.tfvars"
```

The token should be base64-encoded `username:password` for the CrewAI registry. Do you have the registry credentials?

> DEVELOPER

i do, would this apply to kubernetes secrets aswell?

> AGENT

Yes! The Terraform already creates the Kubernetes secret. When you run:

```bash
terraform apply -var="helm_registry_token=<your-token>"
```

It creates `crewai-registry-secret` in the `crewai-dev` namespace as a `kubernetes.io/dockerconfigjson` secret that pods can use via `imagePullSecrets`.

The flow:
1. You provide `helm_registry_token` (base64 of `username:password`)
2. Terraform creates `kubernetes_secret.helm_registry` → `crewai-registry-secret`
3. Pods reference it via `global.imagePullSecrets` in values.yaml

To generate the token:
```bash
echo -n "username:password" | base64
```

Then apply:
```bash
terraform apply -var="helm_registry_token=[REDACTED]="
```

> DEVELOPER

what do i need to do to apply the secret

> AGENT

To apply the secret, you need to run Terraform with the registry credentials:

```bash
cd environments/dev/terraform

terraform apply \
  -var="crewai_registry_username=YOUR_USERNAME" \
  -var="crewai_registry_password=YOUR_PASSWORD"
```

Or if you have the credentials in a `.tfvars` file or environment variables:

```bash
# Using environment variables
export TF_VAR_crewai_registry_username="YOUR_USERNAME"
export TF_VAR_crewai_registry_password="YOUR_PASSWORD"
terraform apply

# Or using a tfvars file
terraform apply -var-file="secrets.tfvars"
```

Since the secret is being renamed from `crewai-registry-secret` to `docker-registry`, Terraform will:
1. Create the new `docker-registry` secret
2. Delete the old `crewai-registry-secret` (if it exists in state)

If this is a fresh apply, it will just create the new secret.

> DEVELOPER

Failed to pull image "registry.crewai.com/proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai-enterprise-platform:0.15.2": rpc error: code = DeadlineExceeded desc = failed to pull and unpack image "registry.crewai.com/proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai-enterprise-platform:0.15.2": failed to resolve reference "registry.crewai.com/proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai-enterprise-platform:0.15.2": failed to do request: Head "https://registry.crewai.com/v2/proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai-enterprise-platform/manifests/0.15.2": dial tcp 162.159.137.43:443: i/o timeout

> AGENT

This is a network connectivity issue - the GKE nodes can't reach `registry.crewai.com` (timeout on port 443). This is typically caused by:

1. **Private GKE cluster without Cloud NAT** - Nodes can't reach external internet
2. **Firewall rules** - Egress blocked to external registries
3. **VPC Service Controls** - Restricting external access

To diagnose, you can check:

```bash
# Check if Cloud NAT exists for the VPC
gcloud compute routers list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke

# Check firewall rules allowing egress
gcloud compute firewall-rules list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke --filter="direction=EGRESS"

# Test from a pod (if you have one running)
kubectl run test-net --rm -it --image=busybox --restart=Never -- wget -O- --timeout=5 https://registry.crewai.com
```

The fix depends on your cluster setup - if it's a private cluster, you'll need Cloud NAT configured on the VPC to allow nodes to reach external registries. This would typically be in your platform infrastructure (kix-platform-infrastructure), not in this service repo.

> DEVELOPER

Failed to pull image "registry.crewai.com/proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai-enterprise-platform:0.15.2": rpc error: code = DeadlineExceeded desc = failed to pull and unpack image "registry.crewai.com/proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai-enterprise-platform:0.15.2": failed to resolve reference "registry.crewai.com/proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai-enterprise-platform:0.15.2": failed to do request: Head "https://registry.crewai.com/v2/proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai-enterprise-platform/manifests/0.15.2": dial tcp 162.159.137.43:443: i/o timeout

> AGENT

The nodes can't reach the external registry. For a private GKE cluster, you need **Cloud NAT** to allow egress. 

Quick check - run this to see if NAT is configured:

```bash
gcloud compute routers list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke --format="table(name,region,nats)"
```

If no NAT exists, you'll need to create one in your platform infrastructure:

```hcl
# Cloud Router
resource "google_compute_router" "router" {
  name    = "kd-ix-eur-dev-router"
  project = "kd-ix-eur-dev-gke"
  region  = "europe-west1"
  network = "your-vpc-name"
}

# Cloud NAT
resource "google_compute_router_nat" "nat" {
  name                               = "kd-ix-eur-dev-nat"
  project                            = "kd-ix-eur-dev-gke"
  router                             = google_compute_router.router.name
  region                             = "europe-west1"
  nat_ip_allocate_option             = "AUTO_ONLY"
  source_subnetwork_ip_ranges_to_nat = "ALL_SUBNETWORKS_ALL_IP_RANGES"
}
```

This would typically go in **kix-platform-infrastructure**, not this service repo, since it's cluster-level networking.

---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Artifact Registry: Remote Repository (mirror for registry.crewai.com)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n# Secret for registry password (required for AR remote repository upstream auth)\nresource \"google_secret_manager_secret\" \"crewai_registry_password\" {\n  project   = var.gke_project_id\n  secret_id = \"crewai-registry-password-${var.environment}\"\n\n  replication {\n    auto {}\n  }\n\n  labels = local.labels\n}\n\nresource \"google_secret_manager_secret_version\" \"crewai_registry_password\" {\n  secret=[REDACTED].crewai_registry_password.id\n  secret_data = var.crewai_registry_password\n}\n\n# Grant AR service agent access to read the secret\nresource \"google_secret_manager_secret_iam_member\" \"ar_service_agent\" {\n  project   = var.gke_project_id\n  secret_id = google_secret_manager_secret.crewai_registry_password.secret_id\n  role      = \"roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor\"\n  member    = \"serviceAccount:service-${data.google_project.gke.number}@gcp-sa-artifactregistry.iam.gserviceaccount.com\"\n}\n\n# Remote repository that mirrors registry.crewai.com\nresource \"google_artifact_registry_repository\" \"crewai_mirror\" {\n  project       = var.gke_project_id\n  location      = var.region\n  repository_id = \"crewai-mirror-${var.environment}\"\n  description   = \"Remote repository mirroring registry.crewai.com\"\n  format        = \"DOCKER\"\n  mode          = \"REMOTE_REPOSITORY\"\n\n  remote_repository_config {\n    docker_repository {\n      custom_repository {\n        uri = \"https://registry.crewai.com\"\n      }\n    }\n    upstream_credentials {\n      username_password_credentials {\n        username                = var.crewai_registry_username\n        password_secret_version = google_secret_manager_secret_version.crewai_registry_password.name\n      }\n    }\n  }\n\n  labels = local.labels\n}\n\n# Grant      1→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
```
     2→# CrewAI Image Mirror
     3→#
     4→# Mirrors container images from CrewAI enterprise registry (images.crewai.com)
     5→# to local Artifact Registry. Runs daily via Cloud Scheduler + Cloud Build.
     6→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     7→
     8→locals {
     9→  mirror_repo_name = "crewai-mirror-${var.environment}"
    10→  mirror_sa_email  = "crewai-image-mirror-${var.environment}@${var.gke_project_id}.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
    11→}
    12→
    13→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    14→# Artifact Registry: Mirror Repository
    15→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    16→
    17→resource "google_artifact_registry_repository" "crewai_mirror" {
    18→  project       = var.gke_project_id
    19→  location      = var.region
    20→  repository_id = local.mirror_repo_name
    21→  format        = "DOCKER"
    22→  description   = "Mirrored CrewAI enterprise container images - ${var.environment}"
    23→  labels        = local.labels
    24→
    25→  cleanup_policies {
    26→    id     = "keep-recent-versions"
    27→    action = "KEEP"
    28→    most_recent_versions {
    29→      keep_count = 10
    30→    }
    31→  }
    32→}
    33→
    34→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    35→# Secret Manager: CrewAI Registry Credentials for Cloud Build
    36→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    37→
    38→module "secret_crewai_registry_user" {
    39→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
    40→
    41→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
    42→  environment = var.environment
    43→  secret_id   = "crewai-image-mirror-user-${var.environment}"
    44→  secret_data = var.crewai_registry_username
    45→
    46→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.mirror_sa_email]
    47→  additional_labels         = local.labels
    48→}
    49→
    50→module "secret_crewai_registry_token" {
    51→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
    52→
    53→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
    54→  environment = var.environment
    55→  secret_id   = "crewai-image-mirror-token-${var.environment}"
    56→  secret_data = var.crewai_registry_password
    57→
    58→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.mirror_sa_email]
    59→  additional_labels         = local.labels
    60→}
    61→
    62→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    63→# Service Account: Image Mirror Cloud Build
    64→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

    66→resource "google_service_account" "image_mirror" {
    67→  project      = var.gke_project_id
    68→  account_id   = "crewai-image-mirror-${var.environment}"
    69→  display_name = "CrewAI Image Mirror - ${var.environment}"
    70→  description  = "Service account for Cloud Build image mirroring jobs"
    71→}
    72→
    73→# Cloud Build builder role
    74→resource "google_project_iam_member" "mirror_cloudbuild_builder" {
    75→  project = var.gke_project_id
    76→  role    = "roles/cloudbuild.builds.builder"
    77→  member  = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.image_mirror.email}"
    78→}
    79→
    80→# Logging for Cloud Build
    81→resource "google_project_iam_member" "mirror_logs_writer" {
    82→  project = var.gke_project_id
    83→  role    = "roles/logging.logWriter"
    84→  member  = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.image_mirror.email}"
    85→}
    86→
    87→# Artifact Registry writer for mirror repo
    88→resource "google_artifact_registry_repository_iam_member" "mirror_writer" {
    89→  project    = var.gke_project_id
    90→  location   = var.region
    91→  repository = google_artifact_registry_repository.crewai_mirror.name
    92→  role       = "roles/artifactregistry.writer"
    93→  member     = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.image_mirror.email}"
    94→}
    95→
    96→# Allow platform SA to read from mirror (for pod pulls)
    97→resource "google_artifact_registry_repository_iam_member" "mirror_platform_reader" {
    98→  project    = var.gke_project_id
    99→  location   = var.region
   100→  repository = google_artifact_registry_repository.crewai_mirror.name
   101→  role       = "roles/artifactregistry.reader"
   102→  member     = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}"
   103→}
   104→
   105→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   106→# Cloud Build Trigger
   107→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   108→
   109→resource "google_cloudbuild_trigger" "image_mirror" {
   110→  project     = var.gke_project_id
   111→  name        = "crewai-image-mirror-${var.environment}"
   112→  description = "Sync CrewAI images from enterprise registry - ${var.environment}"
   113→  location    = var.region
   114→
   115→  service_account = google_service_account.image_mirror.id
   116→
   117→  source_to_build {
   118→    uri       = "https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai"
   119→    ref       = "refs/heads/${var.environment == "prod" ? "main" : "develop"}"
   120→    repo_type = "GITHUB"
   121→  }
   122→
   123→  git_file_source {
   124→    path      = "environments/${var.environment}/cloudbuild/image-mirror.yaml"
   125→    uri       = "https://github.com/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai"
   126→    revision  = "refs/heads/${var.environment == "prod" ? "main" : "develop"}"
   127→    repo_type = "GITHUB"
   128→  }
   129→
   130→  substitutions = {
   131→    _REGION         = var.region
   132→    _ENVIRONMENT    = var.environment
   133→    _MIRROR_PROJECT = var.gke_project_id
   134→    _MIRROR_REPO    = local.mirror_repo_name
   135→    _SECURITY_PROJECT = var.security_project_id
   136→  }
   137→}
   138→
   139→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   140→# Cloud Scheduler: Daily Trigger
   141→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   142→
   143→resource "google_cloud_scheduler_job" "image_mirror_daily" {
   144→  project     = var.gke_project_id
   145→  region      = var.region
   146→  name        = "crewai-image-mirror-daily-${var.environment}"
   147→  description = "Triggers daily image sync at 02:00 UTC - ${var.environment}"
   148→  schedule    = "0 2 * * *"
   149→  time_zone   = "UTC"
   150→
   151→  http_target {
   152→    http_method = "POST"
   153→    uri         = "https://cloudbuild.googleapis.com/v1/projects/${var.gke_project_id}/locations/${var.region}/triggers/${google_cloudbuild_trigger.image_mirror.trigger_id}:run"
   154→    body        = base64encode(jsonencode({ branchName = var.environment == "prod" ? "main" : "develop" }))
   155→
   156→    oauth_token {
   157→      service_account_email = google_service_account.image_mirror.email
   158→    }
   159→  }
   160→
   161→  retry_config {
   162→    retry_count          = 3
   163→    min_backoff_duration = "5s"
   164→    max_backoff_duration = "60s"
   165→  }
   166→}
   167→
   168→# Grant scheduler invoker role to the service account
   169→resource "google_project_iam_member" "mirror_scheduler_invoker" {
   170→  project = var.gke_project_id
   171→  role    = "roles/cloudbuild.builds.builder"
   172→  member  = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.image_mirror.email}"
   173→}
   174→
   175→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   176→# Outputs
   177→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   178→
   179→output "mirror_repository_url" {
   180→  description = "Full URL for the mirror Artifact Registry repository"
   181→  value       = "${var.region}-docker.pkg.dev/${var.gke_project_id}/${local.mirror_repo_name}"
   182→}
   183→
   184→output "mirror_trigger_id" {
   185→  description = "Cloud Build trigger ID for manual invocation"
   186→  value       = google_cloudbuild_trigger.image_mirror.trigger_id
   187→}
   188→
```

```
     1→################################################################################
     2→# CrewAI Image Mirror - Cloud Build Pipeline (dev)
     3→#
     4→# Pulls the CrewAI Helm chart from OCI registry, extracts image references,
     5→# and mirrors all images to Google Artifact Registry.
     6→#
     7→# Triggered daily by Cloud Scheduler at 02:00 UTC.
     8→################################################################################
     9→
    10→substitutions:
    11→  _REGION: europe-west1
    12→  _ENVIRONMENT: dev
    13→  _MIRROR_PROJECT: kd-ix-eur-dev-gke
    14→  _MIRROR_REPO: crewai-mirror-dev
    15→  _SECURITY_PROJECT: kd-ix-eur-dev-security
    16→  _HELM_REGISTRY: registry.crewai.com
    17→  _IMAGE_REGISTRY: images.crewai.com
    18→  _CHART_PATH: crewai/stable
    19→  _CHART_NAME: crewai-platform
    20→
    21→steps:
    22→  # ============================================================================
    23→  # Step 1: Pull Helm chart and extract image references
    24→  # ============================================================================
    25→  - name: 'alpine/helm:latest'
    26→    id: 'extract-images'
    27→    entrypoint: sh
    28→    args:
    29→      - -c
    30→      - |
    31→        set -e
    32→        echo "=== CrewAI Image Mirror - ${_ENVIRONMENT} ==="
    33→        echo "Timestamp: $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
    34→
    35→        # Authenticate to CrewAI Helm OCI registry
    36→        echo "Authenticating to Helm registry: ${_HELM_REGISTRY}"
    37→        echo "$$CREWAI_TOKEN" | helm registry login ${_HELM_REGISTRY} \
    38→          --username "$$CREWAI_USER" --password-stdin
    39→
    40→        # Pull the Helm chart
    41→        echo "Pulling chart: oci://${_HELM_REGISTRY}/${_CHART_PATH}/${_CHART_NAME}"
    42→        helm pull oci://${_HELM_REGISTRY}/${_CHART_PATH}/${_CHART_NAME} \
    43→          --destination /workspace
    44→
    45→        # Extract chart archive
    46→        echo "Extracting chart..."
    47→        tar -xzf /workspace/${_CHART_NAME}-*.tgz -C /workspace
    48→
    49→        # Display chart version
    50→        CHART_VERSION=$(grep '^version:' /workspace/${_CHART_NAME}/Chart.yaml | awk '{print $2}')
    51→        echo "Chart version: $CHART_VERSION"
    52→
    53→        # Template the chart to extract all image references
    54→        echo "Templating chart to extract image references..."
    55→        helm template crewai /workspace/${_CHART_NAME} \
    56→          --set global.imageRegistry=${_IMAGE_REGISTRY} \
    57→          2>/dev/null | \
    58→          grep -oE "${_IMAGE_REGISTRY}/[^\"'[:space:]]+" | \
    59→          sort -u > /workspace/images-to-sync.txt
    60→
    61→        # Also extract from values.yaml directly as backup
    62→        grep -oE 'repository:[[:space:]]*[^[:space:]]+' /workspace/${_CHART_NAME}/values.yaml 2>/dev/null | \
    63→          awk '{print $2}' | grep -v '^$' | \
    64→          while read repo; do
    65→            echo "${_IMAGE_REGISTRY}/$repo:latest"
    66→          done >> /workspace/images-to-sync.txt 2>/dev/null || true
    67→
    68→        # Deduplicate
    69→        sort -u /workspace/images-to-sync.txt -o /workspace/images-to-sync.txt
    70→
    71→        # Count and display
    72→        IMAGE_COUNT=$(wc -l < /workspace/images-to-sync.txt)
    73→        echo ""
    74→        echo "=== Found $IMAGE_COUNT images to sync ==="
    75→        cat /workspace/images-to-sync.txt
    76→        echo ""
    77→    secretEnv: ['CREWAI_USER', 'CREWAI_TOKEN']
    78→
    79→  # ============================================================================
    80→  # Step 2: Mirror images using crane
    81→  # ============================================================================
    82→  - name: 'gcr.io/go-containerregistry/crane:latest'
    83→    id: 'sync-images'
    84→    entrypoint: sh
    85→    args:
    86→      - -c
    87→      - |
    88→        set -e
    89→        echo "=== Starting image sync ==="
    90→
    91→        # Target registry URL
    92→        TARGET="${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${_MIRROR_PROJECT}/${_MIRROR_REPO}"
    93→        echo "Target registry: $TARGET"
    94→
    95→        # Authenticate to source registry
    96→        echo "Authenticating to source: ${_IMAGE_REGISTRY}"
    97→        crane auth login ${_IMAGE_REGISTRY} \
    98→          -u "$$CREWAI_USER" -p "$$CREWAI_TOKEN"
    99→
   100→        # Authenticate to target (uses Cloud Build SA automatically)
   101→        echo "Authenticating to target: ${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev"
   102→        crane auth login ${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev
   103→
   104→        # Track results
   105→        SUCCESS=0
   106→        FAILED=0
   107→        SKIPPED=0
   108→
   109→        # Sync each image
   110→        while IFS= read -r source_image || [ -n "$source_image" ]; do
   111→          [ -z "$source_image" ] && continue
   112→
   113→          # Convert source path to target path
   114→          image_path="${source_image#${_IMAGE_REGISTRY}/}"
   115→          target_image="${TARGET}/${image_path}"
   116→
   117→          echo ""
   118→          echo "--- Syncing: $image_path ---"
   119→
   120→          # Check if image already exists with same digest
   121→          SOURCE_DIGEST=$(crane digest "$source_image" 2>/dev/null || echo "")
   122→          if [ -z "$SOURCE_DIGEST" ]; then
   123→            echo "  ⚠ Source image not found, skipping"
   124→            SKIPPED=$((SKIPPED + 1))
   125→            continue
   126→          fi
   127→
   128→          TARGET_DIGEST=$(crane digest "$target_image" 2>/dev/null || echo "")
   129→          if [ "$SOURCE_DIGEST" = "$TARGET_DIGEST" ]; then
   130→            echo "  ✓ Already synced (digest: ${SOURCE_DIGEST:0:20}...)"
   131→            SKIPPED=$((SKIPPED + 1))
   132→            continue
   133→          fi
   134→
   135→          # Copy with retry logic
   136→          RETRY=0
   137→          MAX_RETRIES=3
   138→          while [ $RETRY -lt $MAX_RETRIES ]; do
   139→            if crane copy "$source_image" "$target_image" 2>&1; then
   140→              echo "  ✓ Synced successfully"
   141→              SUCCESS=$((SUCCESS + 1))
   142→              break
   143→            else
   144→              RETRY=$((RETRY + 1))
   145→              if [ $RETRY -lt $MAX_RETRIES ]; then
   146→                echo "  ⚠ Retry $RETRY/$MAX_RETRIES in 5s..."
   147→                sleep 5
   148→              else
   149→                echo "  ✗ Failed after $MAX_RETRIES attempts"
   150→                FAILED=$((FAILED + 1))
   151→              fi
   152→            fi
   153→          done
   154→        done < /workspace/images-to-sync.txt
   155→
   156→        echo ""
   157→        echo "=== Sync Complete ==="
   158→        echo "  Synced:  $SUCCESS"
   159→        echo "  Skipped: $SKIPPED (already up-to-date or not found)"
   160→        echo "  Failed:  $FAILED"
   161→
   162→        # Fail build if any images failed
   163→        if [ $FAILED -gt 0 ]; then
   164→          echo "ERROR: $FAILED image(s) failed to sync"
   165→          exit 1
   166→        fi
   167→
   168→        echo "All images synced successfully!"
   169→    secretEnv: ['CREWAI_USER', 'CREWAI_TOKEN']
   170→
   171→  # ============================================================================
   172→  # Step 3: Generate sync manifest for audit
   173→  # ============================================================================
   174→  - name: 'gcr.io/cloud-builders/gcloud'
   175→    id: 'generate-manifest'
   176→    entrypoint: sh
   177→    args:
   178→      - -c
   179→      - |
   180→        echo "=== Generating sync manifest ==="
   181→        TARGET="${_REGION}-docker.pkg.dev/${_MIRROR_PROJECT}/${_MIRROR_REPO}"
   182→
   183→        gcloud artifacts docker images list "$TARGET" \
   184→          --include-tags \
   185→          --format="table(package,tags,createTime)" \
   186→          > /workspace/mirror-manifest.txt 2>/dev/null || echo "No images found yet"
   187→
   188→        echo "Mirror contents:"
   189→        cat /workspace/mirror-manifest.txt
   190→        echo ""
   191→        echo "Build completed at: $(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)"
   192→
   193→# Secret references from Secret Manager
   194→availableSecrets:
   195→  secretManager:
   196→    - versionName: projects/${_SECURITY_PROJECT}/secrets/crewai-image-mirror-user-${_ENVIRONMENT}/versions/latest
   197→      env: 'CREWAI_USER'
   198→    - versionName: projects/${_SECURITY_PROJECT}/secrets/crewai-image-mirror-token-${_ENVIRONMENT}/versions/latest
   199→      env: 'CREWAI_TOKEN'
   200→
   201→options:
   202→  logging: CLOUD_LOGGING_ONLY
   203→  machineType: 'E2_MEDIUM'
   204→
   205→timeout: '1800s'
   206→
   207→tags:
   208→  - 'crewai'
   209→  - 'image-mirror'
   210→  - 'dev'
   211→
```

I see the issue. There's **duplicate Artifact Registry repository creation**:

**image-mirror.tf (existing):**
- Creates `crewai-mirror-dev` as a standard DOCKER repo
- Uses Cloud Build to **actively copy** images from `images.crewai.com` → AR
- This works without NAT since Cloud Build has internet access

**main.tf (what I added):**
- Creates `crewai-mirror-dev` as a **remote repository** (pull-through cache)
- This conflicts and also wouldn't work without NAT

I need to:
1. Remove my duplicate code from main.tf
2. Update helm values to use the mirror URL

> AGENT

**Summary of changes:**

| File | Change |
|------|--------|
| [main.tf](environments/dev/terraform/main.tf) | Removed duplicate remote repository code (conflicts with image-mirror.tf), kept secret name as `docker-registry` |
| [values.yaml](environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml) | Changed `imageRegistry` to AR mirror, removed `imagePullSecrets` (Workload Identity handles auth) |

**Architecture now:**
- **image-mirror.tf** creates `crewai-mirror-dev` AR repo + Cloud Build pipeline
- **Cloud Build** syncs images from `images.crewai.com` → AR daily at 02:00 UTC
- **Pods** pull from AR mirror using Workload Identity (no credentials needed)

**Before deploying, you need to:**
1. Run the image mirror Cloud Build trigger to sync images:
   ```bash
   gcloud builds triggers run crewai-image-mirror-dev \
     --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke \
     --region=europe-west1 \
     --branch=develop
   ```

2. Apply terraform (the image-mirror.tf resources):
   ```bash
   cd environments/dev/terraform
   terraform apply
   ```

The `docker-registry` K8s secret in main.tf may no longer be needed since you're using Workload Identity for the AR mirror. Want me to remove it?

> AGENT

Here's a summary of IAM permissions configured by Terraform:

## Platform Service Account (`crewai-platform-dev`)

| Role | Resource | Purpose |
|------|----------|---------|
| `roles/cloudsql.client` | `kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data` project | Connect to Cloud SQL |
| `roles/storage.objectAdmin` | `crewai-data-dev` bucket | Read/write GCS data |
| `roles/storage.objectCreator` | `crewai-logs-dev` bucket | Write logs |
| `roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser` | Self (SA) | Workload Identity binding |
| `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` | GitHub SA | CI/CD impersonation |
| `roles/artifactregistry.writer` | `crewai-dev` AR repo | Push crew images |
| `roles/artifactregistry.reader` | `crewai-dev` AR repo | Pull crew images |
| `roles/artifactregistry.reader` | `crewai-mirror-dev` AR repo | Pull platform images |

## Image Mirror Service Account (`crewai-image-mirror-dev`)

| Role | Resource | Purpose |
|------|----------|---------|
| `roles/cloudbuild.builds.builder` | GKE project | Run Cloud Build |
| `roles/logging.logWriter` | GKE project | Write build logs |
| `roles/artifactregistry.writer` | `crewai-mirror-dev` AR repo | Push mirrored images |
| `roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor` | Registry creds secrets | Read registry credentials |

## What you might need to add manually

If Terraform apply fails with permission errors, your **Terraform service account** needs:
- `roles/iam.serviceAccountAdmin` - Create service accounts
- `roles/artifactregistry.admin` - Create AR repositories  
- `roles/secretmanager.admin` - Create secrets (in security project)
- `roles/cloudbuild.builds.editor` - Create Cloud Build triggers
- `roles/cloudscheduler.admin` - Create Cloud Scheduler jobs

> AGENT

Your Terraform service account needs permission to impersonate the `crewai-image-mirror-dev` service account. Run this:

```bash
# Get your Terraform SA (check your backend config or run terraform with debug)
TF_SA="your-terraform-sa@your-project.iam.gserviceaccount.com"

# Grant impersonation permission
gcloud iam service-accounts add-iam-policy-binding \
  crewai-image-mirror-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com \
  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke \
  --member="serviceAccount:${TF_SA}" \
  --role="roles/iam.serviceAccountUser"
```

Or if you're running Terraform as yourself:

```bash
# For user account
gcloud iam service-accounts add-iam-policy-binding \
  crewai-image-mirror-dev@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com \
  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke \
  --member="user:your-email@domain.com" \
  --role="roles/iam.serviceAccountUser"
```

Then re-run `terraform apply`.

> AGENT

Here are the GCP APIs that need to be enabled for the image mirror and related resources:

```bash
# Required APIs for kd-ix-eur-dev-gke project
gcloud services enable \
  cloudbuild.googleapis.com \
  cloudscheduler.googleapis.com \
  artifactregistry.googleapis.com \
  secretmanager.googleapis.com \
  iam.googleapis.com \
  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke

# For the security project (secrets)
gcloud services enable \
  secretmanager.googleapis.com \
  --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-security
```

Quick check if they're already enabled:

```bash
gcloud services list --enabled --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke \
  --filter="name:(cloudbuild OR cloudscheduler OR artifactregistry OR secretmanager)"
```

The Cloud Build and Cloud Scheduler APIs are most likely to be missing since they're new for this image mirror feature.

> AGENT

The trigger is hardcoded to use `develop` branch. Two options:

**Option 1:** Create a develop branch from current branch:
```bash
git checkout -b develop
git push -u origin develop
```

**Option 2:** Update the terraform to use `main` or current branch temporarily.

Also, Cloud Build needs a GitHub connection configured. Check if it exists:
```bash
gcloud builds connections list --project=kd-ix-eur-dev-gke --region=europe-west1
```

The trigger uses `repoType: GITHUB` which requires either:
- A GitHub App connection (Cloud Build 2nd gen)
- Or the legacy GitHub mirror

Which approach would you prefer?

## YAML Configuration Review Report

### Summary

| File | Issues Found | Severity |

## 1. ArgoCD Application (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/argocd/apps/application.yaml`)

### Critical Issues

**Issue 1: Branch Reference Does Not Match Main Branch (Line 33)**
- **Location:** `sources[1].targetRevision: feature/helm-chart-refinement`
- **Problem:** The ArgoCD Application references branch `feature/helm-chart-refinement` for Helm values. Per the CLAUDE.md documentation, the Application should reference `main` branch for production-ready deployments. The `feature/helm-chart-refinement` branch is a feature branch that should be merged to main before production use.
- **Impact:** If this feature branch is deleted or diverges significantly, ArgoCD will fail to sync.
- **Recommendation:** Change to `main` once feature is merged, or document explicitly that this is intentional for development testing.

### Medium Issues

**Issue 2: Missing `oci://` Prefix in Helm OCI Registry URL (Line 22)**
- **Location:** `sources[0].repoURL: registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable`
- **Problem:** According to the CLAUDE.md documentation, the OCI registry should use the `oci://` prefix: `oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable`. The current value lacks this prefix.
- **Expected:** Based on CLAUDE.md example:
  ```yaml
  - repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable
  ```
- **Note:** ArgoCD may handle this differently depending on version. Verify against ArgoCD 2.x OCI support documentation.

**Issue 3: Chart Name Discrepancy (Line 23)**
- **Location:** `chart: crewai-platform`
- **Problem:** The CLAUDE.md documentation references `crewai-enterprise` as the chart name, but this file uses `crewai-platform`. This inconsistency should be verified.
- **CLAUDE.md states:**
  ```yaml
  chart: crewai-enterprise
  ```
- **Recommendation:** Verify the correct chart name from the OCI registry and update either the Application or documentation accordingly.

### Observations (No Action Required)

- **Line 37:** Hardcoded destination server IP (`https://34.78.246.181`). Consider using the GKE cluster name reference or a more maintainable approach, though IP is valid.
- **Lines 64-75:** `ignoreDifferences` configuration is well-structured and appropriate for GKE environments with HPA and GKE-managed annotations.
- **Line 14-15:** Finalizers are correctly configured to prevent orphaned resources.

---

## 2. Helm Values (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml`)

**Note:** Per your request, I am only commenting on quality, not suggesting value changes.

### Comment Quality Issues

**Issue 1: Inconsistent Comment Block Formatting (Lines 48-98)**
- **Location:** Lines 48-98 (probes and security contexts)
- **Problem:** Large blocks of commented-out code with inconsistent comment markers. Some lines use `#` at the start, others have stray `#` on blank lines (lines 57, 67, 77, 86, 96).
- **Example:**
  ```yaml
  #  failureThreshold: 3
  #                          <-- blank line with just #
    #readinessProbe:
  ```
- **Impact:** Makes the file harder to read and maintain. Stray `#` on blank lines (lines 57, 67, 77, 86, 96, 126, 137, 156, 177, 193) create visual noise.
- **Recommendation:** Either remove the commented-out code entirely, or add explanatory comments about why these sections are disabled and when they should be enabled.

**Issue 2: Commented-Out Sections Without Context (Lines 48-156)**
- **Problem:** Multiple probe and security context configurations are commented out without explanation of why they are disabled or when they should be enabled.
- **Best Practice:** Add a brief comment explaining the rationale, e.g.:
  ```yaml
  # Probes disabled during initial deployment - enable after stable baseline established
  # See: docs/runbooks/enabling-probes.md
  ```

**Issue 3: Stray Hash Marks on Lines 57, 67, 77, 86, 96, 126, 137, 156, 177, 193**
- **Problem:** These lines contain only `#` which appears to be formatting artifacts.
- **Example:** Line 57: `#`
- **Recommendation:** Remove these empty comment lines or replace with proper blank lines.

### Positive Observations

- Clear section headers with descriptive comments
- Proper YAML structure and indentation (2-space consistent)
- Logical grouping of related configuration
- Environment-specific values clearly documented

## 3. Cloud Build Pipeline (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/cloudbuild/image-mirror.yaml`)

### Low Severity Issues

**Issue 1: Use of `latest` Tag for Build Images (Lines 25, 82)**
- **Location:** 
  - Line 25: `alpine/helm:latest`
  - Line 82: `gcr.io/go-containerregistry/crane:latest`
- **Problem:** Using `latest` tags can lead to reproducibility issues. A build today may behave differently than a build tomorrow.
- **Recommendation:** Pin to specific versions for production stability:
  ```yaml
  - name: 'alpine/helm:3.14.0'
  - name: 'gcr.io/go-containerregistry/crane:v0.19.0'
  ```

**Issue 2: Unicode Emoji Characters in Shell Output (Lines 124, 130, 140, 147, 149)**
- **Location:** Lines containing warning and checkmark symbols
- **Problem:** Using emoji/unicode characters in shell scripts can cause issues with certain logging systems or terminals that do not support UTF-8.
- **Examples:**
  - Line 124: `echo "  ⚠ Source image not found, skipping"`
  - Line 130: `echo "  ✓ Already synced"`
- **Recommendation:** Consider using ASCII alternatives for broader compatibility:
  ```
  [WARN], [OK], [FAIL]
  ```
- **Impact:** Low - most modern systems handle UTF-8 fine, but can cause issues in log aggregation.

### Positive Observations

- Excellent documentation with clear section headers
- Proper error handling with retry logic (lines 136-153)
- Build failure on sync failures (lines 163-166)
- Secret management via Secret Manager (lines 194-199)
- Appropriate timeout setting (1800s = 30 minutes)
- Good use of substitution variables for environment-specific configuration
- Audit trail generation in step 3

# Architecture Review Report: kix-services-crewai

## Executive Summary

This repository implements a multi-environment deployment infrastructure for CrewAI Enterprise Platform on GKE. The architecture demonstrates solid fundamentals with proper separation of concerns between Terraform for infrastructure, ArgoCD for application delivery, and Helm for configuration management. However, there are several architectural inconsistencies, potential single points of failure, and areas for improvement.

---

## 1. Repository Structure and Organization

### Strengths
- Clear environment separation with dedicated directories for dev/qa/prod
- Consistent structure within each environment (argocd/, helm/, terraform/)
- Good documentation in CLAUDE.md and README.md
- Proper use of remote Terraform modules from kix-platform-modules

### Concerns

**ISSUE: Inconsistent dev environment structure**
- Dev has a nested helm structure: `environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml` and `environments/dev/helm/crewai/values-gke.yaml`
- QA/Prod use flat structure: `environments/{env}/helm/values.yaml`
- The ArgoCD application for dev references `$values/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml` while QA/Prod reference `$values/environments/{env}/helm/values.yaml`

**ISSUE: Dev has custom Helm templates**
- `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/` contains gateway, network policy, and other templates
- These are absent from QA/Prod, creating environment parity issues

**RECOMMENDATION:**
- Standardize the helm directory structure across all environments
- If custom templates are needed, they should exist in all environments or be handled via a shared location

---

## 2. Environment Separation Analysis

### Terraform Configuration Differences

| Aspect | Dev | QA | Prod |
|--------|-----|-----|------|
| Kubernetes Provider | Explicit GKE data lookup | Empty `provider "kubernetes" {}` | Empty `provider "kubernetes" {}` |
| Cloud SQL Proxy | Standalone deployment in main.tf | Not present | Not present |
| Image Mirror Pipeline | Present (image-mirror.tf) | Not present | Not present |
| Security Project | `var.security_project_id` | Not defined | Not defined |
| GKE Config Module | NOT used (inline resources) | Used | Used |
| Workload Identity Binding | `crewai-{env}/crewai-{env}-sa` | `crewai-{env}/crewai-platform-sa` | `crewai-{env}/crewai-platform-sa` |
| Pod Security Standards | Not configured | Not configured | Configured in gke_config module |

**CRITICAL ISSUE: Dev uses different service account naming**

In dev Terraform (line 220):
```hcl
member = "serviceAccount:${var.gke_project_id}.svc.id.goog[crewai-${var.environment}/crewai-${var.environment}-sa]"
```

In QA/Prod (line 185):
```hcl
member = "serviceAccount:${var.gke_project_id}.svc.id.goog[crewai-${var.environment}/crewai-platform-sa]"
```

And in dev Helm values:
```yaml
serviceAccount: "crewai-dev-sa"
```

While QA/Prod have:
```yaml
serviceAccount:
  create: false
  name: "crewai-platform-sa"
```

This creates a naming inconsistency that could cause Workload Identity failures.

**ISSUE: QA/Prod have empty Kubernetes provider**

In `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/qa/terraform/main.tf` and prod:
```hcl
provider "kubernetes" {}
```

This requires `KUBECONFIG` to be set externally. Dev correctly uses a GKE data source with explicit authentication. This inconsistency can cause CI/CD issues.

**ISSUE: Missing security_project_id in QA/Prod**

Dev stores secrets in a dedicated security project (`var.security_project_id`), but QA/Prod store secrets in the GKE project itself. This is a security architecture inconsistency.

---

## 3. ArgoCD Application Configuration

### Inconsistencies Detected

**ISSUE: Different namespaces for ArgoCD Applications**

| Environment | Application Namespace |
|-------------|----------------------|
| Dev | `argocd` |
| QA | `crewai-root` |
| Prod | `crewai-root` |

**ISSUE: Different OCI repository URL formats**

Dev (`/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/argocd/apps/application.yaml`):
```yaml
repoURL: registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable  # Missing oci:// prefix
```

QA/Prod:
```yaml
repoURL: oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable  # Has oci:// prefix
```

**ISSUE: Dev references feature branch**
```yaml
targetRevision: feature/helm-chart-refinement
```

QA/Prod reference `main`. This is a significant drift risk.

**ISSUE: Dev uses hardcoded cluster IP**
```yaml
destination:
  server: https://34.78.246.181
```

QA/Prod use:
```yaml
destination:
  server: https://kubernetes.default.svc
```

Hardcoded IPs are fragile and should use in-cluster references or DNS names.

**ISSUE: Inconsistent ignoreDifferences configuration**

Dev ignores GKE NEG annotations:
```yaml
ignoreDifferences:
  - group: ""
    kind: Service
    jsonPointers:
      - /metadata/annotations/cloud.google.com~1neg
```

QA/Prod ignore Secret data:
```yaml
ignoreDifferences:
  - group: ""
    kind: Secret
    jsonPointers:
      - /data
```

This inconsistency suggests different operational experiences across environments.

---

## 4. Helm Values Organization

### Dev Configuration Issues

**ISSUE: Commented-out security contexts and probes**

In `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml`, critical configurations are commented out:
- All liveness/readiness/startup probes
- All podSecurityContext configurations
- All securityContext configurations

This means dev runs without:
- Health monitoring
- Security hardening
- Non-root enforcement

This defeats the purpose of having dev as a pre-production validation environment.

**ISSUE: Different image registry strategy**

Dev uses local Artifact Registry mirror:
```yaml
imageRegistry: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-eur-dev-gke/crewai-mirror-dev"
```

QA/Prod use vendor registry:
```yaml
imageRegistry: "registry.crewai.com"
```

While image mirroring is a good practice, QA/Prod should also use mirrored images for:
- Consistency
- Network isolation
- Supply chain security
- Availability (not dependent on vendor registry)

---

## 5. Cloud Build Pipeline (Image Mirror)

### Design Assessment

The image mirroring pipeline in `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta-ix-services/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/cloudbuild/image-mirror.yaml` is well-designed:

**Strengths:**
- Daily scheduled sync at 02:00 UTC
- Digest comparison to avoid redundant copies
- Retry logic with exponential backoff
- Cleanup policies to manage storage

**Concerns:**

**ISSUE: Only exists for dev environment**

Image mirroring should be implemented for all environments. QA/Prod currently rely directly on `registry.crewai.com` which creates:
- External dependency for deployments
- Potential availability issues
- No air-gap capability

**ISSUE: Cloud Scheduler permission may be insufficient**

The IAM binding at line 169-173:
```hcl
resource "google_project_iam_member" "mirror_scheduler_invoker" {
  project = var.gke_project_id
  role    = "roles/cloudbuild.builds.builder"  # Wrong role
  member  = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.image_mirror.email}"
}
```

Should likely use `roles/cloudbuild.builds.editor` or add `cloudscheduler.jobs.run` permission.

---

## 6. Secret Management Architecture

### Current Design

```
                    +-------------------+
                    | Terraform         |
                    | (generates)       |
                    +---------+---------+
                              |
              +---------------+---------------+
              |                               |
    +---------v---------+         +-----------v-----------+
    | GCP Secret Manager|         | K8s Secret            |
    | (backup/audit)    |         | crewai-credentials    |
    +---------+---------+         +-----------+-----------+
              |                               |
              |                               |
    +---------v---------+         +-----------v-----------+
    | accessor_service_ |         | envFrom:              |
    | accounts = [SA]   |         |   secretRef: ...      |
    +-------------------+         +-----------------------+
```

### Concerns

**ISSUE: Secrets stored in both Secret Manager AND Kubernetes Secrets**

This dual-write pattern creates:
- Secret sprawl (two places to manage)
- Potential sync issues
- Increased attack surface

**RECOMMENDATION:** Use either:
1. External Secrets Operator to sync from Secret Manager to K8s
2. Or Workload Identity + Secret Manager CSI Driver for native mounting

**ISSUE: Helm registry token handling differs between environments**

Dev has `crewai_registry_username` and `crewai_registry_password` variables but QA/Prod have `helm_registry_token`. This is inconsistent.

**ISSUE: Default empty strings for sensitive variables**

All sensitive variables default to empty strings:
```hcl
variable "oauth_client_id" {
  default     = ""
  sensitive   = true
}
```

This allows Terraform to run without errors but creates non-functional deployments. Consider removing defaults or using validation blocks.

---

## 7. IAM and Service Account Design

### Architecture

```
+----------------------------------+
| GCP Service Account              |
| crewai-platform-{env}            |
+----------------------------------+
          |
          | Workload Identity Binding
          v
+----------------------------------+
| K8s Service Account              |
| crewai-{env}-sa (dev)            |
| crewai-platform-sa (qa/prod)     |
+----------------------------------+
          |
          | Used by
          v
+----------------------------------+
| Pods via serviceAccount: field   |
+----------------------------------+
```

### Concerns

**ISSUE: Inconsistent K8s service account naming**

As noted earlier, dev uses `crewai-{env}-sa` while QA/Prod use `crewai-platform-sa`.

**ISSUE: Missing GitHub impersonation IAM in QA/Prod**

Dev has (line 223-227):
```hcl
resource "google_service_account_iam_member" "github_impersonation" {
  service_account_id = google_service_account.crewai_platform.name
  role               = "roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator"
  member             = "serviceAccount:github-kix-services-crewai-${var.environment}@${var.gke_project_id}.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
}
```

This is absent from QA/Prod Terraform, suggesting GitHub Actions CI/CD may not work for those environments.

**ISSUE: Overly broad objectAdmin role on data bucket**

```hcl
resource "google_storage_bucket_iam_member" "data_bucket" {
  role   = "roles/storage.objectAdmin"
}
```

Consider using `objectCreator` + `objectViewer` instead of full admin access per principle of least privilege.

---

## 8. Workload Identity Setup

### Current Implementation

Dev:
```hcl
resource "google_service_account_iam_member" "workload_identity" {
  member = "serviceAccount:${var.gke_project_id}.svc.id.goog[crewai-${var.environment}/crewai-${var.environment}-sa]"
}
```

The K8s service account annotation:
```hcl
annotations = {
  "iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account" = google_service_account.crewai_platform.email
}
```

### Concerns

**ISSUE: Crews namespace service account lacks Workload Identity**

The `crewai-crews-{env}` namespace has a K8s service account (`crewai-crews-sa`) but no Workload Identity binding:

In QA/Prod gke_config:
```hcl
"crewai-crews-${var.environment}" = {
  service_account = {
    create          = true
    name            = "crewai-crews-sa"
    automount_token = false
    # No gcp_service_account = ... 
  }
}
```

This means crew workloads cannot access GCP services via Workload Identity.

**RECOMMENDATION:** Create a dedicated GCP service account for crew workloads with appropriate IAM bindings.

---

## 9. Scalability Assessment

### Current Resource Quotas

| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crews Namespace |
|-------------|-------------------|-----------------|
| Dev | 20 CPU / 40Gi / 50 pods | 40 CPU / 80Gi / 100 pods |
| QA | 30 CPU / 60Gi / 80 pods | 60 CPU / 120Gi / 200 pods |
| Prod | 60 CPU / 150Gi / 150 pods | 80 CPU / 160Gi / 300 pods |

### Concerns

**ISSUE: Cloud SQL Proxy is a single point of failure**

Dev has a standalone Cloud SQL Proxy deployment with 1 replica:
```hcl
spec {
  replicas = 1
  ...
}
```

QA/Prod use sidecar pattern (embedded in each pod). For production reliability:
- Increase replica count for standalone proxy
- Consider PodDisruptionBudget
- Add horizontal pod autoscaling

**ISSUE: No Redis configuration visible**

CrewAI typically requires Redis for caching and Action Cable. No Redis configuration is present in Terraform or Helm values. This could be:
1. Handled elsewhere
2. Missing (bug)
3. Using in-memory (not scalable)

**ISSUE: BuildKit has single replica across all environments**

```yaml
buildkit:
  replicaCount: 1
```

BuildKit is likely a bottleneck for crew image builds. Consider multiple replicas for production.

---

## 10. Potential Single Points of Failure

1. **Cloud SQL Instance** - Single regional instance shared by all databases
2. **Cloud SQL Proxy** - Single replica in dev standalone deployment
3. **BuildKit** - Single replica in all environments
4. **GCS Buckets** - Regional, no cross-region replication configured
5. **ArgoCD** - No explicit HA configuration mentioned
6. **External Dependencies** - Direct dependency on `registry.crewai.com` for QA/Prod

---

## 11. Missing Components and Gaps

### Infrastructure Gaps

1. **No monitoring/alerting configuration** - No Prometheus ServiceMonitor, no alerting rules
2. **No backup strategy for Cloud SQL** - Not visible in Terraform
3. **No disaster recovery plan** - Single region deployment
4. **No Cloud Armor configuration** for QA/Prod (disabled in dev intentionally)
5. **Missing PodDisruptionBudgets** for dev environment

### Security Gaps

1. **No VPC Service Controls** visible
2. **No binary authorization** for container images
3. **No network policies** in dev Terraform (inline resources don't include them)
4. **Certificate management** disabled (`certificateManager.enabled: false`)

### Operational Gaps

1. **No runbook integration** with ArgoCD notifications
2. **No cost allocation tags** on GCP resources
3. **No automatic secret rotation** mechanism

---

## 12. Recommendations Summary

### Critical (Address Immediately)

1. **Standardize service account naming** across all environments
2. **Fix Kubernetes provider configuration** in QA/Prod to use explicit GKE authentication
3. **Update dev ArgoCD application** to reference main branch instead of feature branch
4. **Enable security contexts and probes** in dev environment
5. **Fix ArgoCD repoURL format** - ensure consistent oci:// prefix usage

### High Priority

6. **Implement image mirroring** for QA/Prod environments
7. **Add Workload Identity** to crews namespace service account
8. **Remove hardcoded IP** in dev ArgoCD destination
9. **Add GitHub impersonation IAM** to QA/Prod Terraform
10. **Standardize Helm directory structure** across environments

### Medium Priority

11. **Implement External Secrets Operator** or Secret Manager CSI Driver
12. **Add Redis configuration** if required by the application
13. **Scale BuildKit replicas** for production
14. **Add Cloud Armor** for QA/Prod
15. **Implement certificate management**

### Low Priority

16. **Add monitoring configuration** (ServiceMonitors, alerting)
17. **Document disaster recovery procedures**
18. **Add cost allocation labels**
19. **Implement automatic secret rotation**

## Conclusion

The kix-services-crewai repository demonstrates a thoughtful approach to multi-environment deployment with Terraform and ArgoCD. However, significant inconsistencies between environments undermine the reliability of progressive deployment (dev -> qa -> prod). The most critical issues are:

1. Service account naming inconsistencies that could break Workload Identity
2. Dev environment running without security controls and health probes
3. QA/Prod depending on external image registry while dev uses mirrored images
4. Missing infrastructure components (image mirror, GitHub IAM) in QA/Prod

Addressing the critical and high-priority recommendations will significantly improve the architecture's reliability, security, and maintainability.
